}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-Hdl}: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-Hdl] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\estep{m}{e}{e'}{m'}}
} {
  \cstep{\D}{m}{\ehdl{e}}{\D}{m'}{\ehdl{e'}}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Hdl] {
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\texp{\Gamma}{\Pi}{e}{\tone}{E}}
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\ehdl{e}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We apply \thref{thm:prezexp} (expression preservation)
to (1), (2), and premise (i-b) to get that
(3) $\texp{\Gamma}{\Pi}{e'}{\tone}{E}$ and
(4) $\tmem{\Gamma}{\Pi}{m'}$.
\item Choosing $\Pi'=\Pi$, (4) directly gives us conclusion (ii).
We derive conclusion (iii) as follows and we are done:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Hdl] {
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(3)}] { } {\texp{\Gamma}{\Pi}{e'}{\tone}{E}}
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\ehdl{e'}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-HDone}: We have
$\cstep{\D}{m}{\ehdl{\eret{()}}}{\D}{m}{()}$
and: \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Hdl] {
  \texp{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\eret{()}}{\tone}{E}
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\ehdl{\eret{()}}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item Conclusion (iii) is immediate from \ts{TR-Unit},
and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ChkPF}: We have
$\cstep{\D}{m}{\echk{p}}{\D}{m}{\echk{m[p].\mf{tree}}}$
and: \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Chk] {
  \inferrule*[right=TR-Path] {
    \inferrule* {(1)} {\Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt}
    \and
    \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
  } {
    \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{\ttree}
  }
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\echk{p}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item By (1) and premise (i-b), we have (2) that
$\trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{m[p].\mf{tree}}{\ttree}$.
We derive conclusion (iii) as follows and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Chk] {
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(2)}] { } {
    \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{m[p].\mf{tree}}{\ttree}
  }
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\echk{m[p].\mf{tree}}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ChkPT}: We have:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-ChkPT] {
  m[p].\mf{rerender} = \tt
  \and
  \inferrule* {(1)} {m[p].\mf{spec} = \pair{\C}{v}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\D[\C] = x.\hat{e}}
} {
  \cstep{\D}{m}{\echk{p}}
  {\D}{m \mid m[p].\mf{rerender} = \ff}{\eeval{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\hat{e}[v/x]}}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Chk] {
  \inferrule*[right=TR-Path] {
    \Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt
    \and
    \inferrule* {(3)} {\Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])}
  } {
    \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{\ttree}
  }
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\echk{p}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item By definition, premise (i-b) implies that
$\tmem{\Gamma}{\Pi}{m \mid m[p].\mf{rerender} = \ff}$, i.e.
conclusion (ii) holds and it suffices to show conclusion (iii).
\item By (1) and premise (i-b), we get
(4) $\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\Delta[\C]}$.
Let $\Sigma=\dom(\Delta[\C])$; 
by (2) and premise (i-a), we get (5) that
$\tbod{\Gamma[x \mapsto \Delta[\C]]}{\cdot}{\hat{e}}{\C}{\Sigma}$.
\item We first apply \thref{thm:wkpib} (weakening) to (5)
to get (6) that 
$\tbod{\Gamma[x \mapsto \Delta[\C]]}{\Pi}{\hat{e}}{\C}{\Sigma}$.
Then, \thref{thm:subbod} (substitution) using (4) and (6)
gives (7) that 
$\tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{e}[v/x]}{\C}{\Sigma}$.
\item Note, from (4) and definitions of $\Sigma$ and $\C$,
that (8) $\Sigma \subseteq \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = (\Delta[\C])$.
We can then derive conclusion (iii) as follows and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-EvalS] {
  \inferrule*[right=(7)] { } {
    \tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{e}[v/x]}{\C}{\Sigma}
  }
  \
  \inferrule*[right=(8)] { } {
    \Sigma \subseteq \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\C])
  }
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\eeval{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\hat{e}[v/x]}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ChkLit}: 
We have $\cstep{\D}{m}{\echk{\elit{v}}}{\D}{m}{()}$ and:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Chk] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {
    \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\elit{v}}{\ttree}
  }
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\echk{\elit{v}}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item Conclusion (iii) is immediate from \ts{TR-Unit},
and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ChkNode}: We have
$\cstep{\D}{m}{\echk{\enode{id}{v}{\rho}}}{\D}{m}{\echks{\rho}}$
and: \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Chk] {
  \inferrule*[right=TR-Node] {
    \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}
    \and
    \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho}{[\ttree]}}
  } {
    \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\enode{id}{v}{\rho}}{\ttree}
  }
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\echk{\enode{id}{v}{\rho}}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We derive conclusion (iii) as follows and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Chks] {
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(1)}] { } {
    \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho}{[\ttree]}
  }
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\echks{\rho}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ChkNil}: 
We have $\cstep{\D}{m}{\echks{[]}}{\D}{m}{()}$
and: \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Chks] {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{[]}{[\ttree]}
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\echks{[]}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item Conclusion (iii) is immediate from \ts{TR-Unit},
and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ChkCons}: We have\\
$\cstep{\D}{m}{\echks{\rho :: \rho s}}
{\D}{m}{\echk{\rho} \semi \echks{\rho s}}$ and:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Chks] {
  \inferrule*[right=TR-Cons] {
    \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho}{\ttree}}
    \and
    \inferrule* {(2)} {\trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho s}{[\ttree]}}
  } {
    \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho :: \rho s}{[\ttree]}
  }
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\echks{\rho :: \rho s}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We derive conclusion (iii) as follows and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Seq] {
  \inferrule*[right=TR-Chk] {
    \inferrule*[right=\tn{(1)}] { } {
      \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho}{\ttree}
    }
  } {
    \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\echk{\rho}}{\tone}
  }
  \
  \inferrule*[right=TR-Chks] {
    \inferrule*[right=\tn{(2)}] { } {
      \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho s}{[\ttree]}
    }
  } {
    \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\echks{\rho s}}{\tone}
  }
} {
  \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\echk{\rho} \semi \echks{\rho s}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
We have thus shown that preservation holds
for \emph{any} step.
\end{Proof}
\end{lemma}

\pagebreak

\section{Linear-Typed Runtime}

We extend the runtime language of the previous section
with a linear context, in order to prove termination of realization.

\subsection{Syntax}

We define $\Theta=\{\num{p}\}$ to be a \emph{linear} context
of paths. As a well-formedness condition,
$\Theta$ should not contain duplicates, and 
context composition is defined in the usual linear way.

To maintain soundness, we place a \emph{value restriction}
on some terms, namely:
$$\rho ::= \cdots \mid \echk{u} \mid \echks{u} \mid \esearch{id}{u}
\mid \esearches{id}{u}$$

Correspondingly, we shrink our evaluation context grammar as follows:
$$\E ::= [\cdot] \mid \enode{id}{v}{\E} \mid \E ::_\rho \rho
\mid u ::_\rho \E \mid \E \semi \rho \mid \enew{\phi}{p}{\E}$$

\subsection{Memory}

We define $\tpi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\pi}{p}$ to hold if,
for $\pi.\mf{spec}=\pair{\C}{v}$:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\Pi[p].\mf{spec}=\C$,
\item $\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\Delta[\C]}$,
\item $\dom(\pi.\mf{st}) = \Pi[p].\mf{labs}$,
\item for all $\ell \in \dom(\pi.\mf{st})$,
we have $\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\pi[p].\mf{st}[\ell]}{\Delta[\C][\ell]}$,
\item if $\Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt$, then
$\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\pi.\mf{tree}}{\ttree}$,
\item if instead $\Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \ff$, then $\Theta=\cdot$, and
\item $p \notin \Theta$.
\end{itemize}
Above, $\Theta$ is the set of paths ``owned'' by $p$.

We define $\tmeml{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{m}$ to hold if
there is some series of $\Theta_i$ with $\num{\Theta_i}=\Theta$,
and, for all $p_i \in \dom(m)$,
$\tpi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_i}{m[p_i]}{p_i}$.
We also require that $p_0 \notin \Theta$.

Above, $\Theta$ is the set of all ``owned'' paths in the memory.

\subsection{Intuitionistic Typing}

In some circumstances (namely, those which cannot create a reference
to the expression in question),
we permit the intuitionistic typing of runtime expressions.

Judgment: $\trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho}{\rtp}$
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TI-Path] { 
  \Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt
  \and
  \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
} {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{\mf{Tree}}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TI-Node] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}
  \and
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho}{[\ttree]}
} {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\enode{id}{u}{v}}{\ttree}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TI-Nil] { } {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{[]}{[\rtp]}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TI-Lit] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tstr}
} {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\elit{v}}{\ttree}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TI-Cons] {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho}{\rtp}
  \and
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho s}{[\rtp]}
} {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho :: \rho s}{[\rtp]}
}
\end{mathpar}

\subsection{Linear Typing}

In general, we restrict the occurrence of paths in an expression
via the linear context $\Theta$.

Judgment: $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho}{\rtp}$

\subsubsection{Values and Sequences}

\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Unit] { } {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{()}{\tone}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Nil] { } {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{[]}{[\rtp]}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Cons] {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1}{\rho}{\rtp}
  \and
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_2}{\rho s}{[\rtp]}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1, \Theta_2}{\rho :: \rho s}{[\rtp]}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Node] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}
  \and
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho}{[\ttree]}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\enode{id}{v}{\rho}}{\ttree}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Lit] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tstr}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\elit{v}}{\ttree}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Path] {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{\ttree}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{p}{\ttree}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Seq] {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1}{\rho_1}{\tone}
  \and
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_2}{\rho_2}{\rtp}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1, \Theta_2}{\rho_1 \semi \rho_2}{\rtp}
}
\end{mathpar}

\subsubsection{Initialization}

Note that $\mf{eval}$ and $\mf{new}$ ``own'' their paths
in phase $\mf{Init}$ but not in $\mf{Succ}$.
This corresponds to the fact that expressions at $\phi=\mf{Init}$
eventually \emph{reduce to} $p$, i.e. allow a surrounding expression
to reference them,
whereas expressions at $\phi=\mf{Succ}$ reduce to $()$,
and so do not allow superexpressions to reference their path.

\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Init] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tview}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einit{v}}{\ttree}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Ins] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tview]}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einits{v}}{[\ttree]}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-EvalI] {
  \tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{e}}{\Pi[p]}{\Sigma}
  \and
  \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]]) = \Sigma \cup \Pi[p].\mf{labs}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{\eeval{\mf{Init}}{p}{\hat{e}}}{\ttree}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-EvalS] {
  \tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{e}}{\Pi[p]}{\Sigma}
  \and
  \Sigma \subseteq \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\eeval{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\hat{e}}}{\tone}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-NewI] {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho}{\ttree}
  \and
  \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta,p}{\enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{\rho}}{\ttree}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-NewS] {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho}{\ttree}
  \and
  \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\rho}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\subsubsection{Event Handling}

\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Sr] {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{u}{\ttree}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\esearch{id}{u}}{\tone}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Srs] {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{u}{[\ttree]}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\esearches{id}{u}}{\tone}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Sc] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tattr}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\escan{v}}{\tone}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Scs] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\escans{v}}{\tone}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Hdl] {
  \texp{\Gamma}{\Pi}{e}{\tone}{E}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\ehdl{e}}{\tone}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Chk] {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{u}{\ttree}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\echk{u}}{\tone}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Chks] {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{u}{[\ttree]}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\echks{u}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\subsection{Program Typing}

As before, we embed our previous judgment into a larger one
involving $\Omega$ so as to type partially-evaluated programs.

Judgment: $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\Omega}{\rho}{\rtp}$
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Lift] {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho}{\rtp}
  \and
  \dom(\Delta) = \Omega
} {
  \trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\Omega}{\rho}{\rtp}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Prog] {
  \tprog{\Gamma}{P}{\Omega'}
  \and
  \Delta[\C_0] = \pair{\tone}{\emptyset}
  \and
  \dom(\Delta) = \Omega \cup \Omega' \cup \{\C_0\}
} {
  \trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\Omega}{\eprog{P}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\subsection{Configuration Typing}

Define $\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\Omega}{\D}{m}{\rho}{\rtp}$
to hold if, for $\Theta=\Theta_m,\Theta_\rho$:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\tdef{\Gamma}{\Omega}{\D}$,
\item $\tmeml{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_m}{m}$,
\item $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\rho}{\Omega}{\rho}{\rtp}$, and
\item $\Theta \subseteq \dom(\Pi)$.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Type Soundness, Revisited}

Note that
$\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\Omega}{\D}{m}{\rho}{\rtp}$
implies our old judgment
$\tcfg{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Omega}{\D}{m}{\rho}{\rtp}$,
so progress follows from our old \thref{thm:progcfg}.
It remains to show preservation.

We need some additional lemmas under $\Theta$:

\begin{proposition}[Path context weakening, linear sub-judgment.]
\label{thm:wkpirl}
If\\$\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho}{\rtp}$ and $\Pi \podl \Pi'$,
then $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta}{\rho}{\rtp}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Linear uniqueness of typing.]
\label{thm:linunique}
If $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho}{\rtp}$
and also $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta'}{\rho}{\rtp'}$,
then $\Theta=\Theta'$ and $\rtp = \rtp'$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Uniqueness of memory typing.]
\label{thm:memunique}
If $\tmeml{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{m}$
and also $\tmeml{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta'}{m}$
then $\Pi=\Pi'$ and $\Theta = \Theta'$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Linear context containment.]
\label{thm:contain}
If $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho}{\rtp}$,
then $\Theta \subseteq \dom(\Pi)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{lemma}[Delinearization.]
\label{thm:delin}
If $\trhol{\cdot}{\Pi}{\Theta}{u}{\rtp}$,
where $\rtp=\ttree$ or $\rtp=[\ttree]$,
then $\trhoi{\cdot}{\Pi}{u}{\rtp}$.
\begin{Proof}
By induction on the (linear) typing derivation.

\tb{Case} \ts{TL-Nil}: Immediate by \ts{TI-Nil}.

\tb{Case} \ts{TL-Cons}: We have \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Cons] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1}{u}{\ttree}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_2}{u s}{[\ttree]}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1, \Theta_2}{u :: u s}{[\ttree]}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We apply the inductive hypothesis to (1) and (2)
to get (respectively) that (3) $\trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{u}{\ttree}$
and (4) $\trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{us}{[\ttree]}$.
\item We derive the conclusion as follows:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TI-Cons] {
  \inferrule*[right=(3)] { } {\trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{u}{\ttree}}
  \and
  \inferrule*[right=(4)] { } {\trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{u s}{[\ttree]}}
} {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{u :: u s}{[\ttree]}
}
\end{mathpar}
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{TL-Lit}: We have \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Lit] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tstr}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\elit{v}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We derive the conclusion as follows:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TI-Lit] {
  \inferrule*[right=(1)] { } {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tstr}}
} {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\elit{v}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{TL-Node}: We have \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Node] {
  \inferrule*[right=(1)] { } {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}}
  \and
  \inferrule*[right=(2)] { } {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{u}{[\ttree]}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\enode{id}{v}{u}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We apply the inductive hypothesis to (2)
to get (3) that $\trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{u}{[\ttree]}$.
\item We derive the conclusion as follows:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TI-Node] {
  \inferrule*[right=(1)] { } {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}}
  \and
  \inferrule*[right=(3)] { } {\trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{u}{[\ttree]}}
} {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\enode{id}{v}{u}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{TL-Path}: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Path] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{\ttree}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{p}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item The conclusion is given by (1) and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\end{Proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Hole typing, linear sub-judgment.]
\label{thm:lholerhos}
If $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\E[\rho]}{\rtp}$,
then, for some $\rtp'$ and $\Theta_1,\Theta_2$
such that $\Theta=\Theta_1,\Theta_2$,
we have $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1}{\rho}{\rtp'}$.

\begin{Proof}
By induction on the typing judgment.

In each case, we subcase on whether $\E=[\cdot]$ or not.
If so, we choose $\rtp=\rtp'$ and $\Theta_2=\cdot$,
such that the conclusion follows directly from the premise.

Thus, in the following case-analysis, we assume $\E \neq [\cdot]$.
We only mention those cases which are possible by the grammar of $\E$.

\tb{Case} $\enode{id}{v}{\E}$: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Node] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\E[\rho]}{[\ttree]}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\enode{id}{v}{\E[\rho]}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item The conclusion is immediate from the inductive hypothesis on (1).
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} $\E :: \rho s$: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Cons] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\E[\rho]}{\rtp}}
  \and
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta'}{\rho s}{[\rtp]}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta, \Theta'}{\E[\rho] :: \rho s}{[\rtp]}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item The inductive hypothesis on (1) gives us some $\Theta_a,\Theta_b=\Theta$
such that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_a}{\rho}{\rtp'}$.
\item We choose $\Theta_1=\Theta_a$ and $\Theta_2=\Theta',\Theta_b$.
Since $\Theta_a,\Theta_b=\Theta$, we have $\Theta,\Theta'=\Theta_1,\Theta_2$
and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} $u :: \E$: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Cons] {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{u}{\rtp}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta'}{\E[\rho]}{[\rtp]}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta, \Theta'}{u :: \E[\rho]}{[\rtp]}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item The inductive hypothesis on (1) gives us some $\Theta_a,\Theta_b=\Theta'$
such that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_a}{\rho}{\rtp'}$.
\item We choose $\Theta_1=\Theta_a$ and $\Theta_2=\Theta,\Theta_b$.
Since $\Theta_a,\Theta_b=\Theta'$, we have $\Theta,\Theta'=\Theta_1,\Theta_2$
and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} $\E \semi \rho$: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Seq] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\E[\rho]}{\tone}}
  \and
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta'}{\rho_2}{\rtp}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta, \Theta'}{\E[\rho] \semi \rho_2}{\rtp}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item The inductive hypothesis on (1) gives us some $\Theta_a,\Theta_b=\Theta$
such that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_a}{\rho}{\rtp'}$.
\item We choose $\Theta_1=\Theta_a$ and $\Theta_2=\Theta',\Theta_b$.
Since $\Theta_a,\Theta_b=\Theta$, we have $\Theta,\Theta'=\Theta_1,\Theta_2$
and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} $\enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{\E}$: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-NewI] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\E[\rho]}{\ttree}}
  \and
  \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta,p}{\enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{\E[\rho]}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item The inductive hypothesis on (1) gives us some $\Theta_a,\Theta_b=\Theta$
such that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_a}{\rho}{\rtp'}$.
\item We choose $\Theta_1=\Theta_a$ and $\Theta_2=\Theta_b,p$.
Since $\Theta_a,\Theta_b=\Theta$, we have $\Theta,p=\Theta_1,\Theta_2$
and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} $\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\E}$: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-NewS] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho}{\ttree}}
  \and
  \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\rho}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item The conclusion is immediate from the inductive hypothesis on (1).
\end{itemize}
\end{Proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Hole typing, linear judgment.]
\label{thm:lholerho}
If $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\Omega}{\E[\rho]}{\rtp}$,
then, for some $\rtp'$ and $\Theta_1,\Theta_2$
such that $\Theta=\Theta_1,\Theta_2$,
we have $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1}{\Omega}{\rho}{\rtp'}$.

\begin{Proof}
By case-analysis of the typing derivation.

\tb{Case} \ts{TR-Prog}: 
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Prog] {
  \tprog{\Gamma}{P}{\Omega'}
  \and
  \Delta[\C_0] = \pair{\tone}{\emptyset}
  \and
  \dom(\Delta) = \Omega \cup \Omega' \cup \{\C_0\}
} {
  \trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\Omega}{\eprog{P}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We must have $\E=[\cdot]$ and $\rho=\eprog{P}$.
\item The conclusion is given by the premise with $\rtp_2=\rtp_1=\tone$
and $\Theta_1=\Theta_2=\cdot$.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{TR-Lift}:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Lift] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\E[\rho]}{\rtp}}
  \and
  \dom(\Delta) = \Omega
} {
  \trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\Omega}{\E[\rho]}{\rtp}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item The conclusion follows directly from \thref{thm:lholerhos} on (1).
\end{itemize}
\end{Proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Hole typing, linear configs.]
\label{thm:lholecfg}
If $\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\Omega}{\D}{m}{\E[\rho]}{\rtp}$,
then, for some $\rtp'$ and $\Theta_1,\Theta_2$
such that $\Theta=\Theta_1,\Theta_2$,
we have $\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1}{\Omega}{\D}{m}{\rho}{\rtp}$.

\begin{Proof}\
\begin{itemize}
\item By definition, we have (1) $\tdef{\Gamma}{\Omega}{\D}$.
\item We also have some split $\Theta_m,\Theta_\rho=\Theta$ such that
(2) $\tmeml{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_m}{m}$ and 
(3) $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\rho}{\Omega}{\E[\rho]}{\rtp}$.
\item By \thref{thm:lholerho} (hole typing) on (3),
we get some $\Theta_{\rho1},\Theta_{\rho2}=\Theta_\rho$
such that (4) $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_{\rho_1}}{\Omega}{\rho}{\rtp}$.
\item We choose $\Theta_1=\Theta_m,\Theta_{\rho1}$ and $\Theta_2=\Theta_{\rho2}$.
Clearly $\Theta=\Theta_1,\Theta_2$.
\item The conclusion follows from (1), (2), and (4), and we are done.
\end{itemize}
\end{Proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Replacement, linear sub-judgment.]
\label{thm:lreplrhos}
If \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2}{\E[\rho]}{\alpha}$,
\item $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1}{\rho}{\alpha'}$,
\item $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1'}{\rho'}{\alpha'}$ for $\Pi' \rpodl \Pi$, and
\item $(\Theta_1' \setminus \Theta_1) \cap \dom(\Pi) = \emptyset$,
\end{enumerate}
then $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\E[\rho']}{\alpha}$.

\begin{Proof}
By induction on the typing premise \emph{(i)}.

In each case, we subcase on whether $\E=[\cdot]$ or not.
If so, \thref{thm:linunique} (typing uniqueness)
on premises \emph{(i)} and \emph{(ii)} gives that $\alpha'=\alpha$
and $\Theta_2=\cdot$, and then the conclusion is immediate from
premise \emph{(iii)}.

Thus, in the following case-analysis, we assume $\E \neq [\cdot]$.
We only mention those cases which are possible by the grammar of $\E$.

\tb{Case} $\enode{id}{v}{\E}$: By premise \emph{(i)}, we have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Node] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2}{\E[\rho]}{[\ttree]}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2}{\enode{id}{v}{\E[\rho]}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item By inductive hypothesis on (2) and premises \emph{(ii)}-\emph{(iv)},
we get (3) that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\E[\rho']}{[\ttree]}$.
\item By \thref{thm:wkpiv} (weakening) on (1), we have (4) that
$\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{v}{[\tattr]}$. We derive the conclusion as follows:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Node] {
  \inferrule*[right=(4)] { } {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{v}{[\tattr]}}
  \and
  \inferrule*[right=(3)] { } {
    \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\E[\rho']}{[\ttree]}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\enode{id}{v}{\E[\rho]}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} $\E :: \rho_\mf{tl}$: Let $\Theta_2=\Theta_2',\Theta_\mf{tl}$.
By premise \emph{(i)}, we have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Cons] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho]}{\rtp}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\mf{tl}}{\rho_\mf{tl}}{[\rtp]}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1, \Theta_2',\Theta_\mf{tl}}{\E[\rho] :: \rho_\mf{tl}}{[\rtp]}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item By inductive hypothesis on (1) and premises \emph{(ii)}-\emph{(iv)},
we get (3) that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho']}{\rtp}$.
\item By \thref{thm:wkpirl} (weakening) on (2), we get (4)
that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_\mf{tl}}{\rho_\mf{tl}}{[\rtp]}$.
\item We want to conclude via the derivation:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Cons] {
  \inferrule*[right=(3)] { } {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho']}{\rtp}}
  \ 
  \inferrule*[right=(4)] { } {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_\mf{tl}}{\rho_\mf{tl}}{[\rtp]}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1', \Theta_2',\Theta_\mf{tl}}{\E[\rho'] :: \rho_\mf{tl}}{[\rtp]}
}
\end{mathpar}
\item It suffices to show that $\Theta_1',\Theta_2$ is well-defined.
\item By premise \emph{(i)} we know that $\Theta_1,\Theta_2$ is well-defined, 
i.e. (4) $\Theta_1 \cap \Theta_2 = \emptyset$. By \thref{thm:contain}
(containment), we also have (5) that $\Theta_2 \subseteq \dom(\Pi)$.
\item It follows from (4), (5), and premise \emph{(iv)} that 
$\Theta_1' \cap \Theta_2 = \emptyset$:
\begin{itemize}
  \item For any $p \in \Theta_1'$, either $p \in \Theta_1$ or not.
  \item If $p \in \Theta_1$, then $p \notin \Theta_2$ by (4).
  \item If $p \notin \Theta_1$, then $p \notin \Pi$ by premise \emph{(iv)}.
  By (5), we again get $p \notin \Theta_2$.
\end{itemize}
\item Thus, $\Theta_1',\Theta_2$ is well-defined and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} $u :: \E$: Let $\Theta_2=\Theta_2',\Theta_\mf{hd}$.
By premise \emph{(i)}, we have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Cons] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\mf{hd}}{u}{\rtp}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho]}{[\rtp]}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1, \Theta_2',\Theta_\mf{hd}}{u :: \E[\rho]}{[\rtp]}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item By inductive hypothesis on (2) and premises \emph{(ii)}-\emph{(iv)},
we get (3) that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho']}{[\rtp]}$.
\item By \thref{thm:wkpirl} (weakening) on (1), we get (4)
$\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_\mf{hd}}{u}{\rtp}$.
\item We want to conclude via the derivation:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Cons] {
  \inferrule*[right=(4)] { } {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_\mf{hd}}{u}{\rtp}}
  \and
  \inferrule*[right=(3)] { } {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho']}{[\rtp]}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2',\Theta_\mf{hd}}{u :: \E[\rho']}{[\rtp]}
}
\end{mathpar}
\item It suffices to show that $\Theta_1',\Theta_2$ is well-defined.
This follows from an argument analogous to the previous case,
and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} $\E \semi \rho''$: Let $\Theta_2=\Theta_2',\Theta_3$.
By premise \emph{(i)}, we have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Seq] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho]}{\tone}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_3}{\rho''}{\rtp}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1, \Theta_2', \Theta_3}{\E[\rho] \semi \rho''}{\rtp}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item By inductive hypothesis on (1) and premises \emph{(ii)}-\emph{(iv)},
we get (3) that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho']}{\tone}$.
\item By \thref{thm:wkpirl} (weakening) on (2), we get (4)
$\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_3}{\rho''}{\rtp}$.
\item We want to conclude via the derivation:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Seq] {
  \inferrule*[right=(3)] { } {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho']}{\tone}}
  \and
  \inferrule*[right=(4)] { } {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_3}{\rho''}{\rtp}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1', \Theta_2', \Theta_3}{\E[\rho'] \semi \rho''}{\rtp}
}
\end{mathpar}
\item It suffices to show that $\Theta_1',\Theta_2$ is well-defined.
This follows from an argument analogous to the previous two cases,
and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} $\enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{\E}$: Let $\Theta_2=\Theta_2',p$.
By premise \emph{(i)}, we have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-NewI] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho]}{\ttree}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2',p}{\enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{\E[\rho]}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item By inductive hypothesis on (1) and premises \emph{(ii)}-\emph{(iv)},
we get (3) that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho']}{\ttree}$.
\item Since $\Pi \podl \Pi'$, (2) gives us (4) that 
$\Pi'[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi'[p]])$.
\item We want to conclude via the derivation:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-NewI] {
  \inferrule*[right=(3)] { } {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2'}{\E[\rho']}{\ttree}}
  \
  \inferrule*[right=(4)] { } {\Pi'[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi'[p]])}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2',p}{\enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{\E[\rho']}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item It remains to be shown that $\Theta_1',\Theta_2$ is well-defined,
given that $\Theta_1',\Theta_2'$ is well-defined by (3).
\item Since $\Theta_1,\Theta_2',p$ is well-defined (by premise \emph{(i)}),
clearly $p \notin \Theta_2'$.
Thus, it suffices to show that $p \notin (\Theta_1' \setminus \Theta_1)$.
\item By (2), clearly $p \in \dom(\Pi)$, so from premise \emph{(iv)}
we conclude $p \notin (\Theta_1' \setminus \Theta_1)$ and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} $\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\E}$: By premise \emph{(i)}, we have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-NewS] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2}{\E[\rho]}{\ttree}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2}{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\E[\rho]}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item By inductive hypothesis on (1) and premises \emph{(ii)}-\emph{(iv)},
we get (3) that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\E[\rho']}{\ttree}$.
\item Since $\Pi \podl \Pi'$, (2) gives us (4) that 
$\Pi'[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi'[p]])$.
\item We conclude via the derivation:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-NewS] {
  \inferrule*[right=(3)] { } {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\E[\rho']}{\ttree}}
  \
  \inferrule*[right=(4)] { } {\Pi'[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi'[p]])}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\E[\rho']}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\
\end{Proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Replacement, linear judgment.]
\label{thm:lreplrho}
If \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2}{\Omega}{\E[\rho]}{\alpha}$,
\item $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1}{\Omega}{\rho}{\alpha'}$,
\item $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1'}{\Omega'}{\rho'}{\alpha'}$ 
for $\Pi' \rpodl \Pi$ and $\Omega' \rpodl \Omega$, and
\item $(\Theta_1' \setminus \Theta_1) \cap \dom(\Pi) = \emptyset$,
\end{enumerate}
then $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\Omega'}{\E[\rho']}{\alpha}$.

\begin{Proof}
By case-analysis on the typing premise \emph{(i)}.

\tb{Case} \ts{TR-Prog}: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Prog] {
  \tprog{\Gamma}{P}{\Omega'}
  \and
  \Delta[\C_0] = \pair{\tone}{\emptyset}
  \and
  \dom(\Delta) = \Omega \cup \Omega' \cup \{\C_0\}
} {
  \trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\Omega}{\eprog{P}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We must have $\E=[\cdot]$, $\rho=\eprog{P}$, and $\Theta_1=\Theta_2=\cdot$.
\item The conclusion is immediate from premise \emph{(iii)}.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{TR-Lift}: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Lift] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2}{\E[\rho]}{\rtp}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\dom(\Delta) = \Omega}
} {
  \trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2}{\Omega}{\E[\rho]}{\rtp}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item \thref{thm:replrhos} (replacement) on (1) gives us (3) that
$\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\E[\rho']}{\rtp}$.
\item By (2) and the fact that $\Omega \podl \Omega'$,
we have (4) that $\Omega' = \dom(\Delta)$.
\item We rederive the conclusion as follows, and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Lift] {
  \inferrule* {(3)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\E[\rho']}{\rtp}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(4)} {\dom(\Delta) = \Omega'}
} {
  \trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\Omega'}{\E[\rho']}{\rtp}
}
\end{mathpar}
\
\end{Proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Replacement, linear configs.]
\label{thm:lreplcfg}
If \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1,\Theta_2}{\Omega}{\D}{m}{\E[\rho]}{\alpha}$,
\item $\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1}{\Omega}{\D}{m}{\rho}{\alpha'}$,
\item $\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1'}{\Omega'}{\D'}{m'}{\rho'}{\alpha'}$ 
for $\Pi' \rpodl \Pi$ and $\Omega' \rpodl \Omega$, and
\item $(\Theta_1' \setminus \Theta_1) \cap \dom(\Pi) = \emptyset$,
\end{enumerate}
then $\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\Omega'}{\D'}{m'}{\E[\rho']}{\alpha}$.

\begin{Proof}\
\begin{itemize}
\item For some $\Theta_m$ and $\Theta_\rho$ with $\Theta_m,\Theta_\rho=\Theta_1$,
premise \emph{(ii)} gives us (1) that $\tmeml{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_m}{m}$
and (2) that $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\rho}{\Omega}{\rho}{\alpha'}$.
\item Similarly, for $\hat{\Theta}_m,\hat{\Theta}_\rho=\Theta_1,\Theta_2$,
premise \emph{(i)} says (3) that $\tmeml{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{\Theta}_m}{m}$
and (4) that $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{\Theta}_\rho}{\Omega}{\E[\rho]}{\alpha}$.
\item By \thref{thm:memunique} (uniqueness) on (1) and (3), we have that
$\Theta_m=\hat{\Theta}_m$.
\item Since $\hat{\Theta}_m,\hat{\Theta}_\rho=\Theta_1,\Theta_2$
and $\Theta_m,\Theta_\rho=\Theta_1$,
we also get $\hat{\Theta}_\rho=\Theta_\rho,\Theta_2$.
\item Thus, (4) gives us (5) that
$\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\rho,\Theta_2}{\Omega}{\E[\rho]}{\alpha}$.
\item From premise \emph{(iii)}, by definition, we have 
(6) that $\tdef{\Gamma}{\Omega'}{\D'}$,
(7) that $\tmeml{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_m'}{m'}$, and
(8) that $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_\rho'}{\Omega'}{\rho'}{\alpha'}$
(for a split $\Theta_1'=\Theta_m',\Theta_\rho'$).
\item By (6) and (7), in order to show the conclusion, it suffices to show
(9) that $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_\rho',\Theta_2}{\Omega'}{\E[\rho']}{\alpha}$.
\item We apply \thref{thm:lreplrho} (replacement)
using (5), (2), (8), and premise \emph{(iv)}
to get precisely (9), and we are done.
\end{itemize}
\end{Proof}
\end{lemma}

We are now ready to state and prove preservation under $\Theta$.

\begin{lemma}[Preservation of configurations, linear extension.]
\label{thm:prezcfgl}
If \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\Omega}{\D}{m}{\rho}{\rtp}$ and
\item $\cstep{\D}{m}{\rho}{\D'}{m'}{\rho'}$,
\end{enumerate}
then there exist some $\Pi' \rpodl \Pi$, $\Theta' \supseteq \Theta$,
and $\Omega' \supseteq \Omega$ such that
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta'}{\Omega'}{\D'}{m'}{\rho'}{\rtp}$ and
\item $(\Theta' \setminus \Theta) \cap \Pi = \emptyset$.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Proof}
Premise \emph{(i)} gives us the following three sub-premises:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(i-\alph*)]
\item $\tdef{\Gamma}{\Omega}{\D}$,
\item $\tmeml{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{m}$, and
\item $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\Omega}{\rho}{\rtp}$.
\end{enumerate}

Likewise, we want to show the following three sub-conclusions:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(i-\alph*)]
\item $\tdef{\Gamma}{\Omega'}{\D'}$,
\item $\tmeml{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta'}{m'}$, and
\item $\trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta'}{\Omega'}{\rho'}{\rtp}$.
\end{enumerate}

Unless otherwise specified, we choose $\Omega'=\Omega$,
$\Pi'=\Pi$, and $\Theta'=\Theta$, such that conclusion \emph{(ii)}
is trivial, and conclusions (i-a)
and (i-b) are immediate from premises (i-a) and (i-b)
when $\D'=\D$ and $m'=m$, respectively.

In most cases, we elide mention of $\Omega$
and work over an implicitly \ts{TR-Lift}ed sub-judgment,
i.e. premise (i-c) gives us $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho}{\rtp}$,
and for conclusion (i-c) it suffices to show
$\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta'}{\rho'}{\rtp}$.

We proceed by induction over the step judgment in premise \emph{(ii)}.

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-Ctx}: We have \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-Ctx] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\cstep{\D}{m}{\rho}{\D'}{m'}{\rho'}}
} {
  \cstep{\D}{m}{\E[\rho]}{\D'}{m'}{\E'[\rho]}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item Premise \emph{(i)} says that 
$\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\Omega}{\D}{m}{\E[\rho]}{\rtp}$.
\item \thref{thm:lholecfg} (hole typing) on \emph{(i)} gives us,
for some $\Theta_1,\Theta_2=\Theta$, that
(2) $\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_1}{\Omega}{\D}{m}{\rho}{\rtp'}$.
\item We apply the inductive hypothesis to (1) and (2) to get
some $\Pi' \rpodl \Pi$, $\Omega' \rpodl \Omega$,
and $\Theta_1' \supseteq \Theta_1$ such that
(3) $\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1'}{\Omega'}{\D'}{m'}{\rho'}{\rtp'}$
and (4) $(\Theta_1' \setminus \Theta_1) \cap \Pi = \emptyset$.
\item Finally, we apply \thref{thm:lreplcfg} (replacement) to
premise \emph{(i)}, substituting (3) in for (2) and using (4), to get 
$\tcfgl{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\Theta_1',\Theta_2}{\Omega'}{\D'}{m'}{\E[\rho']}{\rtp}$,
i.e. conclusion \emph{(i)}
\item (4) gives us that
$(\Theta_1',\Theta_2) \setminus (\Theta_1,\Theta_2) \cap \dom(\Pi)=\emptyset$,
so conclusion \emph{(ii)} also holds and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-Seq}:
We have $\cstep{\D}{m}{() \semi \rho}{\D}{m}{\rho}$ and:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Seq] {
  \inferrule*[right=TL-Unit] { } {
    \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\rho_1}{\tone}
  }
  \and
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho_2}{\rtp}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta}{\rho_1 \semi \rho_2}{\rtp}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item Conclusion (i-c) is directly given by (1) and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-Prog}: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-Prog] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\pstep{\D}{P}{P'}{\D'}}
} {
  \cstep{\D}{m}{\eprog{P}}{\D'}{m}{\eprog{P'}}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Prog] {
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\tprog{\Gamma}{P}{\Omega}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(3)} {\Delta[\C_0] = \pair{\tone}{\emptyset}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(4)} {\dom(\Delta) = \Omega \cup \Omega_D \cup \{\C_0\}}
} {
  \trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\Omega_D}{\eprog{P}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item Premise (i-a), in this case,
says (5) that $\tdef{\Gamma}{\Omega_D}{\D}$.
We apply \thref{thm:prezprog} (program preservation)
to (1), (2), and (5) 
to get some $\Omega' \podl \Omega$
and $\Omega_D' \rpodl \Omega_D$ such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $\tprog{\Gamma}{P'}{\Omega'}$,
\item $\tdef{\Gamma}{\Omega_D'}{\D'}$, and
\item $\Omega' \cup \Omega_D' = \Omega \cup \Omega_D$.
\end{enumerate}
\item Choose our new $\Omega$ to be $\Omega_D'$, such that
conclusion (i-a) is given by (b).
\item By (4) and (c), we get (6) that 
$\dom(\Delta) = \Omega' \cup \Omega_D' \cup \{\C_0\}$.
We derive conclusion (i-c) as follows, and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Prog] {
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(a)}] { } {\tprog{\Gamma}{P'}{\Omega'}}
  \
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(3)}] { } {\Delta[\C_0] = \pair{\tone}{\emptyset}}
  \:
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(6)}] { } {
    \dom(\Delta) = \Omega' \cup \Omega_D' \cup \{\C_0\}
  }
} {
  \trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\Omega_D'}{\eprog{P'}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-PDone}: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-PDone] {
  \D' = \D[\C_0 \mapsto x.\eret{v}]
  \\
  m' = m[p_0 \mapsto \{\mf{spec}: \pair{\C_0}{()}, \mf{st}: \cdot,
  \mf{tree}: \cdot, \mf{rerender}: \mf{ff}\}]
} {
  \cstep{\D}{m}{\eprog{\eret{v}}}{\D'}{m'}{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p_0}{\einit{v}}}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Prog] {
  \inferrule*{
    (1)
  } {
    \tprog{\Gamma}{\eret{v}}{\emptyset}
  }
  \
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\Delta[\C_0] = \pair{\tone}{\emptyset}}
  \
  \inferrule*{(3)} {
    \dom(\Delta) = \emptyset \cup \Omega \cup \{\C_0\}
  }
} {
  \trhop{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\Omega}{\eprog{\eret{v}}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item From (1) we further derive:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TP-Lift] {
  \inferrule*[right=TE-Ret] {
    \inferrule* {(4)} {\tval{\Gamma}{\cdot}{v}{\tview}}
  } {
    \texp{\Gamma}{\cdot}{\eret{v}}{\tview}{\epsilon}
  }
} {
  \tprog{\Gamma}{\eret{v}}{\emptyset}\ (1)
}
\end{mathpar}
\item Choose $\Omega' = \Omega \cup \C_0$.
To show conclusion (i-a) given premise (i-a), 
it thus suffices to show (5)
that $\tbod{\Gamma[x_0 \mapsto \Delta[\C_0]]}{\cdot}
{\eret{v}}{\C_0}{\dom(\Delta[\C_0])}$.
\item By freshness of $x_0$, $\Gamma \po \Gamma[x_0 \mapsto \tone]$.
We can thus apply \thref{thm:wkgav} (weakening) to (4)
to get (6) that $\tval{\Gamma[x_0 \mapsto \tone]}{\cdot}{v}{\tview}$.
(5) is then given by (2) and the following derivation:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TB-Lift] {
  \inferrule*[right=TE-Ret] {
    \inferrule*[right=\tn{(6)}] { } {
      \tval{\Gamma[x_0 \mapsto \tone]}{\cdot}{v}{\tview}
    }
  } {
    \texp{\Gamma[x_0 \mapsto \tone]}{\cdot}{\eret{v}}{\tview}{\epsilon}
  }
} {
  \tbod{\Gamma[x_0 \mapsto \tone]}{\cdot}{\eret{v}}{\C_0}{\emptyset}
}
\end{mathpar}
\item Choose $\Pi'=\Pi[p_0 \mapsto \{
  \mf{spec}: \C_0,
  \mf{labs}: \emptyset,
  \mf{tree}: \ff
\}]$. Since $p_0$ is fresh, we have $\Pi \podl \Pi'$.
Then by definition of $m'$, to show conclusion (i-b),
it suffices to show that $\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{()}{\Delta[\C_0]}$.
This is immediate from (2) and \ts{TV-Unit}.
\item Finally, we want to show conclusion (i-c).
By (3) and definition of $\Omega'$, we have $\dom(\Delta)=\Omega'$,
so it suffices to show (7) 
$\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\cdot}{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p_0}{\einit{v}}}{\tone}$.
\item We weaken (4) by \thref{thm:wkpiv} to get (8) that
$\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{v}{\tview}$.
Also, by (4) and definition of $\Pi'$,
we have (9) that $\Pi'[p_0].\mf{labs} = \emptyset = \dom(\Delta[\Pi'[p_0]])$.
We can now derive (7) as follows, and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-New] {
  \inferrule*[right=TL-Init] {
    \inferrule*[right=\tn{(8)}] { } {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{v}{\tview}}
  } {
    \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\cdot}{\einit{v}}{\ttree}
  }
  \
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(9)}] { } {
    \Pi'[p_0].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi'[p_0]])
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\cdot}{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p_0}{\einit{v}}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ISpec}: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-ISpec] {
  p\ \mf{fresh}
  \and 
  \D[\C]=x.\hat{e}
  \\
  m'=m[p \mapsto \{
    \mf{spec}=\pair{\C}{v}, 
    \mf{st}: \cdot, 
    \mf{tree}: \cdot, 
    \mf{rerender}: \mf{ff}
  \}]
} {
  \cstep{\D}{m}{\einit{\pair{\C}{v}}}{\D}{m'}{\eeval{\mf{Init}}{p}{\hat{e}[v/x]}}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Init] {
  \inferrule*[right=TV-Spec] {
    \inferrule* {(1)} {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\Delta[\C]}}
  } {
    \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\pair{\C}{v}}{\tview}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einit{\pair{\C}{v}}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item Choose $\Pi'=\Pi[p \mapsto \{
  \mf{spec}: \C,
  \mf{labs}: \emptyset,
  \mf{tree}: \ff
\}]$. $p$ is fresh, so $\Pi \podl \Pi'$.
\item Choose $\Theta'=p$. Since $p$ is fresh, $p \notin \dom(\Pi)$,
so conclusion \emph{(ii)} holds.
\item To show conclusion (i-b) given premise (i-b),
it suffices to show that $\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{v}{\Delta[\C]}$.
This is immediate from (1) and \thref{thm:wkpiv} (weakening).
\item By premise (i-a), we get
$\tbod{\Gamma[x \mapsto \Delta[\C]]}{\cdot}{\hat{e}}{\C}{\dom(\Delta[\C])}$.
Say $\Sigma = \dom(\Delta[\C])$.
We apply lemmas as follows to get (2):
\begin{align*}
&\tbod{\Gamma[x \mapsto \Delta[\C]]}{\cdot}{\hat{e}}{\C}{\Sigma}
&&(\tn{Premise (i-a)})\\
\Rightarrow\quad
&\tbod{\Gamma[x \mapsto \Delta[\C]]}{\Pi}{\hat{e}}{\C}{\Sigma}
&&(\tn{\thref{thm:wkpib}, weakening})\\
\Rightarrow\quad
&\tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{e}[v/x]}{\C}{\Sigma}
&&(\tn{\thref{thm:subbod}, substitution with (1)})\\
\Rightarrow\quad&(2)\quad
\tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\hat{e}[v/x]}{\C}{\Sigma}
&&(\tn{\thref{thm:wkpib}, weakening})
\end{align*}
\item Since $\Pi'[p].\mf{labs} = \emptyset$,
we also have (3) that 
$\dom(\Delta[\Pi'[p]]) = \Sigma \cup \Pi'[p].\mf{labs}$.
We can now derive conclusion (i-c) as follows, 
noting that $\Pi'[p]=\C$, and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-EvalI] {
  \inferrule*[right=(2)] { } {
    \tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\hat{e}[v/x]}{\C}{\Sigma}
  }
  \
  \inferrule*[right=(3)] { } {
    \dom(\Delta[\C]) = \Sigma \cup \Pi'[p].\mf{labs}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{p}{\eeval{\mf{Init}}{p}{\hat{e}[v/x]}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-IText}:
We have $\cstep{\D}{m}{\einit{\vtxt{v}}}{\D}{m}{\elit{v}}$
and: \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Init] {
  \inferrule*[right=TV-Text] {
    \inferrule* {(1)} {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tstr}}
  } {
    \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\vtxt{v}}{\mf{View}}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einit{\vtxt{v}}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We derive conclusion (i-c) as follows, and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Lit] {
  \inferrule*[right=(1)] { } {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tstr}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\elit{v}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ITag}:
We have 
$\cstep{\D}{m}{\einit{\vtag{v_1}{v_2}}}{\D}{m}{\enode{id}{v_1}{\einits{v_2}}}$
and: \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Init] {
  \inferrule*[right=TV-Tag] {
    \inferrule* {(1)} {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v_1}{[\mf{Attr}]}}
    \and
    \inferrule* {(2)} {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v_2}{[\mf{View}]}}
  } {
    \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\vtag{v_1}{v_2}}{\mf{View}}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einit{\vtag{v_1}{v_2}}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We derive conclusion (i-c) as follows, and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Node] {
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(1)}] { } {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}}
  \and
  \inferrule*[right=TL-Ins] {
    \inferrule*[right=\tn{(2)}] { } {
      \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v_2}{[\mf{View}]}
    }
  } {
    \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einits{v_2}}{[\ttree]}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\enode{id}{v_1}{\einits{v_2}}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-INil}:
We have $\cstep{\D}{m}{\einits{[]}}{\D}{m}{[]}$ and:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Ins] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{[]}{[\tview]}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einits{[]}}{[\ttree]}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item Conclusion (iii) is given by \ts{TL-Nil}:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Nil] { } {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{[]}{[\ttree]}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ICons}:
We have
$\cstep{\D}{m}{\einits{v :: vs}}{\D}{m}{\einit{v} :: \einits{vs}}$
and: \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Ins] {
  \inferrule*[right=TV-Cons] {
    \inferrule* {(1)} {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tview}}
    \and
    \inferrule* {(2)} {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{vs}{[\tview]}}
  } {
    \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v :: vs}{[\tview]}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einits{v :: vs}}{[\ttree]}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We derive conclusion (iii) as follows, and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Cons] {
  \inferrule*[right=TL-Init] {
    \inferrule*[right=(1)] { } {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tview}}
  } {
    \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einit{v}}{\ttree}
  }
  \inferrule*[right=TL-Ins] {
    \inferrule*[right=(2)] { } {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{vs}{[\tview]}}
  } {
    \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einits{vs}}{[\ttree]}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einit{v} :: \einits{vs}}{[\ttree]}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-Eval}: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-Eval] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\bstep{m}{\hat{e}}{\phi}{p}{\hat{e}'}{m'}}
} {
  \cstep{\D}{m}{\eeval{\phi}{p}{\hat{e}}}{\D}{m'}{\eeval{\phi}{p}{\hat{e}'}}
}
\end{mathpar}
We subcase on whether $\phi=\mf{Init}$ or $\phi=\mf{Succ}$.

If $\phi=\mf{Init}$, we also have:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-EvalI] {
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{e}}{\Pi[p]}{\Sigma}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(3)} {\dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]]) = \Sigma \cup \Pi[p].\mf{labs}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{\eeval{\mf{Init}}{p}{\hat{e}}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We apply \thref{thm:prezbodi} (body preservation at $\phi=\mf{Init}$)
using (1), (2), and premise (i-b) 
to get some $\Pi' \rpodl \Pi$ and $\Sigma' \subseteq \Sigma$ such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item $\tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\hat{e}'}{\Pi'[p]}{\Sigma'}$,
\item $\tmem{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{m'}$, and
\item $\Pi'[p].\mf{labs} \cup \Sigma' = \Pi[p].\mf{labs} \cup \Sigma$.
\end{enumerate}
\item Choose this $\Pi'$ as our new $\Pi$. 
Conclusion (i-b) is immediate from (b).
\item By (c) and (3), we get (4) that
$\dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]]) = \Sigma' \cup \Pi'[p].\mf{labs}$.
Since $\Pi \podl \Pi'$, it must be that 
$\Pi[p].\mf{spec}=\Pi'[p].\mf{spec}$,
so we conclude this subcase by deriving conclusion (i-c) as follows:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-EvalI] {
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(a)}] { } {
    \tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{\hat{e}'}{\Pi'[p]}{\Sigma'}
  }
  \
  \inferrule*[right=(4)] { } {
    \dom(\Delta[\Pi'[p]]) = \Sigma' \cup \Pi'[p].\mf{labs}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi'}{p}{\eeval{\mf{Init}}{p}{\hat{e}'}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
If instead $\phi=\mf{Succ}$, we have:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-EvalS] {
  \inferrule* {(5)} {\tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{e}}{\Pi[p]}{\Sigma}}
  \
  \inferrule* {(6)} {\Sigma \subseteq \Pi[p].\mf{labs}}
  \
  \inferrule* {(7)} {\Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\eeval{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\hat{e}}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item It follows from \thref{thm:progbods}
(body \emph{progress} at $\phi=\mf{Succ}$),
applied to (5), (6), and premise (i-b), that $m' = m$. 
Choose $\Pi'=\Pi$; conclusion (i-b) is trivial.
\item We apply \thref{thm:prezbods} 
(body \emph{preservation} at $\phi=\mf{Succ}$)
using (1), (5), and premise (i-b) 
to get some $\Sigma' \subseteq \Sigma$ with
(8) $\tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{e}'}{\Pi[p]}{\Sigma'}$.
\item Since $\Sigma' \subseteq \Sigma$,
it follows from (6) and (7) that (9)
$\Sigma' \subseteq \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])$.
We can now derive conclusion (i-c) as follows:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-EvalS] {
  \inferrule*[right=(6)] { } {
    \tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{e}'}{\Pi[p]}{\Sigma}
  }
  \
  \inferrule*[right=(9)] { } {
    \Sigma \subseteq \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\eeval{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\hat{e}'}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-New}: We have
$\cstep{\D}{m}{\eeval{\phi}{p}{\eret{v}}}
{\D}{m}{\enew{\phi}{p}{\einit{v}}}$.

We again subcase on whether $\phi=\mf{Init}$ or $\phi=\mf{Succ}$.

If $\phi=\mf{Init}$, we have:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-EvalI] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {
    \tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\eret{v}}{\Pi[p]}{\emptyset}
  }
  \
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]]) = \emptyset \cup \Pi[p].\mf{labs}}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{\eeval{\mf{Init}}{p}{\eret{v}}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We continue inversion on (1):
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TB-Lift] {
  \inferrule*[right=TE-Ret] {
    \inferrule* {(3)} {
      \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tview}
    }
  } {
    \texp{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\eret{v}}{\tview}{\epsilon}
  }
} {
  \tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\eret{v}}{\Pi[p]}{\emptyset}\ (1)
}
\end{mathpar}
\item We now derive conclusion (i-c) as follows:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-NewI] {
  \inferrule*[right=TL-Init] {
    \inferrule*[right=(3)] { } {
      \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tview}
    }
  } {
    \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einit{v}}{\ttree}
  }
  \and
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(2)}] { } {
    \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{\enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{\einit{v}}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
If instead $\phi=\mf{Succ}$, we have:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-EvalS] {
  \inferrule* {(4)} {
    \tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\eret{v}}{\Pi[p]}{\emptyset}
  }
  \and
  \inferrule* {(5)} {
    \emptyset \subseteq \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\eeval{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\hat{e}}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item (4) is identical to (1), so we perform the same inversion
to obtain (3) in this subcase as well.
\item We derive conclusion (i-c) as follows, 
thus satisfying all subcases:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-NewS] {
  \inferrule*[right=TL-Init] {
    \inferrule*[right=\tn{(3)}] { } {
      \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tview}
    }
  } {
    \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\einit{v}}{\ttree}
  }
  \and
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(5)}] { } {
    \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\einit{v}}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-NewI}: We have
$\cstep{\D}{m}{\enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{u}}
{\D}{m'}{p}$
with $m'$ defined to be $m \mid m[p].\mf{tree} = u$,
and: \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-NewI] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {
    \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\rho}{u}{\ttree}
  }
  \
  \inferrule* {(2)} {
    \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\rho,p}{\enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{u}}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We choose $\Theta'=\Theta$, but we \emph{redistribute} $\Theta'$
between the memory and the expression,
such that $\Theta_m' = \Theta_m,\Theta_\rho$ and $\Theta_\rho'=\{p\}$.
\item Within the memory, we distribute $\Theta_m$ across paths
exactly as before, and we assign $\Theta_\rho$ to $p$.
\item Finally, choose $\Pi' = \Pi \mid \Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt$.
By (2), we already have $p \in \dom(\Pi)$,
so to show conclusion (i-b), it suffices to show
that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\rho}{u}{\ttree}$, which is given by (1).
\item Conclusion (i-c) follows from the derivation:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Path] {
  \inferrule*[right=TI-Path] {
    \inferrule* { } {\Pi'[p].\mf{tree} = \tt}
    \and
    \inferrule*[right=(2)] { } {
      \Pi'[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi'[p]])
    }
  } {
    \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{\ttree}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{p}{\ttree}
}
\end{mathpar}
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-NewS}: We have
$\cstep{\D}{m}{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{u}}{\D}{m'}{()}$,
where $m'$ is defined as $m \mid m[p].\mf{tree} = u$,
and: \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-New] {
  \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\rho}{u}{\ttree}}
  \and
  \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\rho}{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{u}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We again choose $\Theta'=\Theta$,
but redistribute such that $\Theta_m'=\Theta_m,\Theta_\rho$
and $\Theta_\rho'=\emptyset$.
\item Within the memory, we distribute $\Theta_m$ across paths
exactly as before, and we assign $\Theta_\rho$ to $p$.
\item Finally, choose $\Pi' = \Pi \mid \Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt$.
By (2), we already have $p \in \dom(\Pi)$,
so to show conclusion (i-b), it suffices to show
that $\trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\Theta_\rho}{u}{\ttree}$, which is given by (1).
\item Conclusion (i-c) follows from \ts{TL-Unit}
(under empty $\Theta_\rho'$) and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ChkPT}: We have:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-ChkPT] {
  m[p].\mf{rerender} = \tt
  \and
  \inferrule* {(1)} {m[p].\mf{spec} = \pair{\C}{v}}
  \and
  \inferrule* {(2)} {\D[\C] = x.\hat{e}}
} {
  \cstep{\D}{m}{\echk{p}}
  {\D}{m \mid m[p].\mf{rerender} = \ff}{\eeval{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\hat{e}[v/x]}}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TL-Chk] {
  \inferrule*[right=TI-Path] {
    \Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt
    \and
    \inferrule* {(3)} {\Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])}
  } {
    \trhos{\Gamma}{\Pi}{p}{\ttree}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\echk{p}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item By definition, premise (i-b) implies that
$\tmem{\Gamma}{\Pi}{m \mid m[p].\mf{rerender} = \ff}$, i.e.
conclusion (i-b) holds and it suffices to show conclusion (i-c).
\item By (1) and premise (i-b), we get
(4) $\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\Delta[\C]}$.
Let $\Sigma=\dom(\Delta[\C])$; 
by (2) and premise (i-a), we get (5) that
$\tbod{\Gamma[x \mapsto \Delta[\C]]}{\cdot}{\hat{e}}{\C}{\Sigma}$.
\item We first apply \thref{thm:wkpib} (weakening) to (5)
to get (6) that 
$\tbod{\Gamma[x \mapsto \Delta[\C]]}{\Pi}{\hat{e}}{\C}{\Sigma}$.
Then, \thref{thm:subbod} (substitution) using (4) and (6)
gives (7) that 
$\tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{e}[v/x]}{\C}{\Sigma}$.
\item Note, from (4) and definitions of $\Sigma$ and $\C$,
that (8) $\Sigma \subseteq \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = (\Delta[\C])$.
We can then derive conclusion (i-c) as follows and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-EvalS] {
  \inferrule*[right=(7)] { } {
    \tbod{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\hat{e}[v/x]}{\C}{\Sigma}
  }
  \
  \inferrule*[right=(8)] { } {
    \Sigma \subseteq \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\C])
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\eeval{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\hat{e}[v/x]}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ChkNode}: We have
$\cstep{\D}{m}{\echk{\enode{id}{v}{\rho}}}{\D}{m}{\echks{\rho}}$
