\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Chks] {
  \inferrule*[right=\tn{(1)}] { } {
    \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho}{[\ttree]}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\echks{\rho}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ChkNil}: 
We have $\cstep{\D}{m}{\echks{[]}}{\D}{m}{()}$
and: \begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Chks] {
  \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{[]}{[\ttree]}
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\echks{[]}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item Conclusion (iii) is immediate from \ts{TL-Unit},
and we are done.
\end{itemize}

\tb{Case} \ts{ER-ChkCons}: We have\\
$\cstep{\D}{m}{\echks{\rho :: \rho s}}
{\D}{m}{\echk{\rho} \semi \echks{\rho s}}$ and:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Chks] {
  \inferrule*[right=TI-Cons] {
    \inferrule* {(1)} {\trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho}{\ttree}}
    \and
    \inferrule* {(2)} {\trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho s}{[\ttree]}}
  } {
    \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\rho :: \rho s}{[\ttree]}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\echks{\rho :: \rho s}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{itemize}
\item We derive conclusion (iii) as follows and we are done:
\end{itemize}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TL-Seq] {
  \inferrule*[right=TL-Chk] {
    \inferrule*[right=\tn{(1)}] { } {
      \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho}{\ttree}
    }
  } {
    \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\echk{\rho}}{\tone}
  }
  \
  \inferrule*[right=TL-Chks] {
    \inferrule*[right=\tn{(2)}] { } {
      \trhoi{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\rho s}{[\ttree]}
    }
  } {
    \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\echks{\rho s}}{\tone}
  }
} {
  \trhol{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\cdot}{\echk{\rho} \semi \echks{\rho s}}{\tone}
}
\end{mathpar}

\hl{TODO}: we need to show expression and body evaluations
don't affect $\Theta$ (i.e. linear memory typing is preserved).
\end{Proof}
\end{lemma}

\subsection{Realization}

We define the $\mf{realize}$ meta-function to ``unfold'' a memory into
a concrete tree structure as follows:
\begin{align*}
\mf{realize}_m(\elit{v}) &= \elit{v}\\
\mf{realize}_m(\enode{id}{v}{u}) &= \enode{id}{v}{\mf{realize}_m(u)}\\
\mf{realize}_m(p) &= \mf{realize}_m(m[p].\mf{tree})\\
\mf{realize}_m([]) &= []\\
\mf{realize}_m(u :: us) &= \mf{realize}_m(u) :: \mf{realize}_m(us)
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{Direct Proof Approach}

\hl{...}

\subsubsection{Language-Based Formulation}

\begin{align*}
\text{Concrete tree } t &::= \elit{v} \mid \enode{id}{v}{t}
\mid \mf{realize}(u) \mid [] \mid t :: t\\
\text{Concrete tree value } t_v &::= \elit{v} \mid \enode{id}{v}{t_v}
\mid [] \mid t_v :: t_v\\
\text{Concrete tree type } \alpha^* &::= \ttree^* \mid [\ttree]^*\\
\text{Evaluation context } \E &::= [\cdot] \mid \enode{id}{v}{\E}
\mid \E :: t \mid t_v :: \E
\end{align*}

We augment our runtime
tree-typing judgments with a measure $n \in \mathbb{N}$ of the size
of the syntax tree:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TN-Lit] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tstr}
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \cdot \Vdash^1_\Delta \elit{v}: \ttree
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TN-Node] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta \Vdash^n_\Delta v: [\ttree]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta \Vdash^{n+1}_\Delta \enode{id}{v}{u}: \ttree
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TN-Path] {
  \Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt
  \and
  \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi p \Vdash^1_\Delta p: \ttree
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TN-Nil] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \cdot \Vdash^1_\Delta []: [\ttree]
}\and
\inferrule*[right=TN-Cons] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_1 \Vdash^{n_1}_\Delta u_1 : \ttree
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_2 \Vdash^{n_2}_\Delta u_2 : [\ttree]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_1,\Theta_2
  \Vdash^{n_1+n_2+1}_\Delta 
  u_1 :: u_2: [\ttree]
}
\end{mathpar}

We define a typing judgment on concrete trees,
which includes a measure of the number of unrealized syntax nodes:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TC-Lit] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tstr}
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \cdot \Vdash^0_\Delta \elit{v}: \ttree^*
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TC-Node] {
  \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta \Vdash^n_\Delta t: [\ttree]^*
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta \Vdash^{n}_\Delta \enode{id}{v}{t}: \ttree^*
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TC-Real] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta \Vdash^n_\Delta u: \alpha
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta
  \Vdash^{n}_\Delta \mf{realize}(u): \alpha^*
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TC-Nil] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \cdot \Vdash^0_\Delta []: [\ttree]^*
}\and
\inferrule*[right=TC-Cons] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_1 \Vdash^{n_1}_\Delta t_1 : \ttree^*
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_2 \Vdash^{n_2}_\Delta t_2 : [\ttree]^*
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_1,\Theta_2
  \Vdash^{n_1+n_2}_\Delta 
  t_1 :: t_2: [\ttree]^*
}
\end{mathpar}

We define $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Sigma \semi \Theta \vDash_\Delta \pi: p$ 
to hold if,
for $\pi.\mf{spec}=\pair{\C}{v}$:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\Pi[p].\mf{spec}=\C$,
\item $\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\Delta[\C]}$,
\item $\dom(\pi.\mf{st}) = \Pi[p].\mf{labs}$,
\item for all $\ell \in \dom(\pi.\mf{st})$,
we have $\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{\pi.\mf{st}[\ell]}{\Delta[\C][\ell]}$,
\item if $\Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt$ and $p \notin \Sigma$, then
$\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta \Vdash^n_\Delta \pi.\mf{tree} : \ttree$
(for some $n$),
\item if instead $\Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \ff$ or $p \in \Sigma$, then $\Theta=\cdot$, and
\item $p \notin \Theta$.
\end{itemize}
($\Sigma$ here is the context of ``seen'' paths in a realization.)

We define $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Sigma \semi \Theta \vDash_\Delta m$ 
to hold if
there is some series of $\Theta_i$ with $\num{\Theta_i}=\Theta$,
and, for all $p_i \in \dom(m)$,
$\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Sigma \semi \Theta_i \vDash_\Delta m[p_i]: p_i$.
We also require that $p_0 \notin \Theta$ and $\Theta \cap \Sigma = \emptyset$.

Define 
$\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Sigma \semi \Theta \vdash_\Delta^n \pair{m}{t}: \alpha$
to abbreviate that 
$\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Sigma \semi \Theta_m \vDash_\Delta m$
and $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_t \Vdash^n_\Delta t: \alpha$,
where $\Theta=\Theta_m,\Theta_t$.

% TODO: domain-equality constraint? (add to linear definition too?)

Finally, we define an operational semantics to realize concrete trees:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=EC-Ctx] {
  t \mapsto_m t'
} {
  \E[t] \mapsto_m \E[t']
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{align*}
\mf{realize}(\elit{v}) &\mapsto_m \elit{v} &&\textsc{EC-Lit}\\
\mf{realize}(\enode{id}{v}{u}) &\mapsto_m \enode{id}{v}{\mf{realize}(u)}
&&\textsc{EC-Node}\\
\mf{realize}(p) &\mapsto_m \mf{realize}(m[p].\mf{tree})
&&\textsc{EC-Path}\\
\mf{realize}([]) &\mapsto_m []
&&\textsc{EC-Nil}\\
\mf{realize}(u :: us) &\mapsto_m \mf{realize}(u) :: \mf{realize}(us)
&&\textsc{EC-Cons}\\
\end{align*}

\begin{proposition}[Hole typing, realization.]
\label{thm:realhole}
If $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_e,\Theta_h
\Vdash^{n_e + n_h}_\Delta \E[t]: \alpha^*$,
then $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_h
\Vdash^{n_h}_\Delta t: \alpha'^*$
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Replacement, realization.]
\label{thm:realrepl}
If:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_e,\Theta_h
\Vdash^{n_e + n_h}_\Delta \E[t]: \alpha^*$,
\item $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_h
\Vdash^{n_h}_\Delta t: \alpha'^*$,
\item $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_h'
\Vdash^{n_h'}_\Delta t: \alpha'^*$, and
\item $\Theta_e \cap \Theta'_h = \emptyset$,
\end{enumerate}
then $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_e,\Theta_h'
\Vdash^{n_e + n_h'}_\Delta \E[t']: \alpha^*$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{lemma}[Progress of realization.]
If $\cdot \semi \Pi \semi \Sigma \semi \Theta 
\vdash^n_\Delta \pair{m}{t}: \alpha$,
then either $t=t_v$ or $t \mapsto_m t'$ for some $t'$.

\begin{Proof}
By induction on the typing derivation.

The only non-trivial case is when $t=\mf{realize}(p)$,
in which case we need to show that $m[p].\mf{tree}$
is well-defined, but this is given by the premise
that $\Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt$ and the well-typing of the memory $m$.
\end{Proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Preservation of realization.]
If \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Sigma \semi \Theta
\vdash_\Delta^n \pair{m}{t}: \alpha$ and
\item $t \mapsto_m t'$,
\end{enumerate}
then, for some $\Theta'$, $\Sigma'$, and $n'$, we have
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Sigma' \semi \Theta'
\vdash_\Delta^{n'} \pair{m}{t'}: \alpha$, and
\item either: \begin{enumerate}[label=(ii-\alph*)]
  \item $\Theta' \subset \Theta$, or
  \item $\Theta' = \Theta$ and $n' < n$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Proof}
By induction on the derivation of the step judgment.

\tb{Case} \ts{EC-Ctx}: (\hl{informal})
\begin{itemize}
\item \thref{thm:realhole}: hole is well-typed
\item and the $\Theta_h$ that types it is disjoint from $\Theta_m$,
so $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Sigma \semi \Theta_m,\Theta_h
\vdash_\Delta^{n_h} \pair{m}{t}: \alpha'$
\item IH gives $\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Sigma' \semi \Theta''
\vdash_\Delta^{n'_h} \pair{m}{t'}: \alpha'$
\item subcases: either (a) $\Theta'' \subset \Theta_m,\Theta_h$,
or (b) $\Theta''=\Theta_m,\Theta_h$ and $n'_h < n_h$
\item in subcase (a): since $\Theta_m,\Theta_e,\Theta_h$ is well-typed,
we get $\Theta_m',\Theta_e,\Theta_h'$ is too,
i.e. $\Theta_h' \cap \Theta_e=\emptyset$
\item \thref{thm:realrepl} gives us our conclusion
(choosing $\Theta'=\Theta_m',\Theta_h',\Theta_e$, $\Sigma'$,
and $n'=n_e+n_h'$)
\item in subcase (b), clearly $\Theta_h \cap \Theta_e=\emptyset$
\item so \thref{thm:realrepl} gives us our typing conclusion,
$\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_e,\Theta_h \Vdash^{n_e+n_h'}_\Delta
\E[t']: \alpha$
\item choose $n'=n_e+n_h'$, satisfying conclusion \emph{(ii-b)}
and we are done
\end{itemize}


\tb{Case} \ts{EC-Lit}: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TC-Real] {
  \inferrule*[right=TN-Lit] {
    \inferrule* {(1)} {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tstr}}
  } {
    \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \cdot \Vdash^1_\Delta \elit{v}: \ttree
  }
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \cdot
  \Vdash^{1}_\Delta \mf{realize}(\elit{v}): \ttree^*
}
\end{mathpar}
Choosing $\Theta'=\cdot$, $\Sigma'=\Sigma$, and $n'=0$, 
we derive the conclusion as follows:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TC-Lit] {
  \inferrule*[right=(1)] { } {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{\tstr}}
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \cdot \Vdash^0_\Delta \elit{v}: \ttree^*
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{EC-Node}: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TC-Real] {
  \inferrule*[right=TN-Node] {
    \inferrule* {(1)} {\tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}}
    \and
    \inferrule* {(2)} {
      \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta
      \Vdash^n_\Delta u: [\ttree]
    }
  } {
    \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta 
    \Vdash^{n+1}_\Delta \enode{id}{v}{u}: \ttree
  }
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta
  \Vdash^{n+1}_\Delta \mf{realize}(\enode{id}{v}{u}): \ttree^*
}
\end{mathpar}
Choose $\Theta'=\Theta$, $\Sigma'=\Sigma$,
and $n'=n$ to satisfy conclusion \emph{(ii-b)}.
We derive the typing conclusion as follows:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TC-Node] {
  \inferrule*[right=(1)] { } {
    \tval{\Gamma}{\Pi}{v}{[\tattr]}
  }
  \ 
  \inferrule*[right=TC-Real] {
    \inferrule*[right=(2)] { } {
      \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta
      \Vdash^n_\Delta u: [\ttree]
    }
  } {
    \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta
    \Vdash^n_\Delta \mf{realize}(u): [\ttree]^*
  }
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta
  \Vdash^{n}_\Delta \enode{id}{v}{\mf{realize}(u)}: \ttree^*
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{EC-Path}: We have
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TC-Real] {
  \inferrule*[right=TN-Path] {
    \inferrule* {(1)} {\Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt}
    \and
    \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \dom(\Delta[\Pi[p]])
  } {
    \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi p \Vdash^1_\Delta p: \ttree
  }
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi p
  \Vdash^{1}_\Delta \mf{realize}(p): \ttree^*
}
\end{mathpar}
Choose $\Theta'=\Theta_m$.
By (1) and the memory typing premise (which implies $p \notin \Sigma$),
there is some split $\Theta_m=\Theta_p,\Theta_m'$
such that (2)
$\Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_p \Vdash^n_\Delta m[p].\mf{tree}: \ttree$.
Split $\Theta'$ such that $\Theta_m'$ is assigned to the memory
and $\Theta_p$ to the term.
Furthermore, choose $\Sigma'=\Sigma,p$ and $n'=n$.
Since $\Theta' \subset \Theta$, we satisfy conclusion \emph{(ii-a)}.

We assign $\Theta_p'=\cdot$ and the rest of $\Theta_m'$ as before,
but the well-typedness
of the memory still holds because $p \in \Sigma$.

Finally, the well-typedness of the term is given as follows:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TC-Real] {
  \inferrule*[right=(2)] { } {
    \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_p 
    \Vdash^n_\Delta m[p].\mf{tree}: \ttree
  }
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \semi \Theta_p
  \Vdash^{n}_\Delta \mf{realize}(m[p].\mf{tree}): \ttree^*
}
\end{mathpar}

\tb{Case} \ts{EC-Nil}: Choose $n'=0$; easy.

\tb{Case} \ts{EC-Cons}: Choose $n'=n-1$; easy.

\end{Proof}
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[Termination of realization.]
If $\tcfgl{\cdot}{\Pi}{\Theta_m}{\Omega}{\D}{m}{\rho}{\rtp}$
and $\Pi[p_0].\mf{tree} = \tt$,
then $\mf{realize}_m(p_0)$ terminates.

\begin{Proof}
By definition of the typing premise,
we have $\tmeml{\cdot}{\Pi}{\Theta_m}{m}$,
where $p_0 \notin \Theta_m$.
It follows by definition that (1)
$\cdot \semi \Pi \semi \cdot \semi \Theta_m \vDash_\Delta m$.
From the latter premise, 
\hl{as long as $\mf{tree}=\tt \Rightarrow \mf{labs}$ full},
we can derive
(2) that $\cdot \semi \Pi \semi p_0 \Vdash^1_\Delta p_0: \ttree$.
Now, progress and preservation applied repeatedly to (1) and (2)
give us that $\mf{realize}$ never ``gets stuck''.

Choose (lexicographic) measure $\pair{|\Theta|}{n} \in \mathbb{N}^2$.
Preservation tells us that this measure is strictly decreasing
across steps, and since $\mathbb{N}^2$ is lexicographically well-ordered,
$\mf{realize}$ must terminate and we are done.
\end{Proof}
\end{theorem}

\section{Translation Workshopping}

\subsection{Syntax}

We recap the syntax of the user-level and runtime languages:
\begin{align*}
\text{Value } v &::= x \mid () \mid s \mid \lambda x.e \mid [] \mid v :: v\\
&\quad\mid \C \mid \vtxt{v} \mid \vtag{v}{v}
\mid \vattr{v}{v} \mid \vclick{v}\\
&\quad\mid \mgrey{\pair{\C}{v}} \mid \mgrey{\vset{\ell}{p}}\\
\text{Expression } e &::= \eret{v} \mid \elet{x}{e}{e} \mid v\ v \\
&\quad\mid \ecase{v}{[]}{e_1}{x_\mf{hd} :: x_\mf{tl}}{e_2}\\
\text{Body }\hat{e} &::= e \mid \estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}} \mid \elet{x}{e}{\hat{e}}\\
\text{Program } P &::= e \mid \elet{x}{e}{P} \mid \elet{\C(x)}{\hat{e}}{P}\\
\text{Type } \ptp &::= \tone \mid \tstr \mid \ptp \arrow{E} \ptp
\mid [\ptp] \\
&\quad\mid \tview \mid \tattr
\mid \tcomp{\ptp} \mid \tset{\ptp}\\
\text{Effect } E &::= \epsilon \mid \emut
\end{align*}
For the runtime language, we assume a FGCBV formulation
(as opposed to the previous CBV formulation):
\begin{align*}
\text{Runtime values } u
&::= x \mid ()_\rho \mid []_\rho \mid u ::_\rho u
\mid \elit{v} \mid \enode{id}{v}{u}
\mid p\\
\text{Runtime expressions } \rho
&::= \eret{u} \mid \elet{x}{\rho}{\rho}\\
&\quad\mid
\einit{v} \mid \einits{v} \mid \escan{v} \mid \escans{v}\\
&\quad\mid \echk{u} \mid \echks{u}
\mid \esearch{id}{u} \mid \esearches{id}{u}\\
&\quad\mid \eeval{\phi}{p}{\hat{e}} \mid \enew{\phi}{p}{u}
\mid \ehdl{e} \mid \eprog{P}\\
\text{Runtime type } \rtp &::= \tone_\rho \mid \mf{Tree}
\mid [\ttree]
\end{align*}

\subsection{Target Language}

The following target language is sufficient to translate 
user-level programs (but not the runtime language):
\begin{align*}
\text{Target values } v
&::= x \mid () \mid s \mid \mf{true} \mid \mf{false} \mid
\lambda x.e \mid [] \mid v :: v \mid r \mid (v, v)\\
&\quad\mid \vtxt{v} \mid \vtag{v}{v} \mid \vattr{v}{v} \mid \vclick{v}\\
\text{Target expressions } e
&::= \eret{v} \mid \elet{x}{e}{e} \mid v\ v\\
&\quad\mid \eref{v} \mid \eget{v} \mid \eset{v}{v}\\
&\quad\mid \ecase{v}{[]}{e_1}{x :: x}{e_2}\\
\text{Target type } A &::= \tone \mid \tstr \mid A \rightarrow A \mid [A]
\mid \tview \mid \tattr\\
&\quad\mid \tref{A} \mid A \times A \mid \tbool
\end{align*}
Typing rules and operational semantics are entirely standard:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=T-Var] {
  x: A \in \Gamma
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v x: A
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Unit] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v (): \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Str] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v s: \tstr
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-True] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v \mf{true}: \tbool
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-False] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v \mf{false}: \tbool
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Lam] {
  \Gamma, x: A_1 \semi \Sigma \vdash_e e: A_2
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v \lambda x.e: A_1 \rightarrow A_2
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Nil] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v []: [A]
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Cons] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_1: A
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_2: [A]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_1 :: v_2: [A]
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Loc] {
  r: A \in \Sigma
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v r: \tref{A}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Pair] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_1: A_1
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_2: A_2
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v (v_1, v_2): A_1 \times A_2
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Text] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v: \tstr
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v \vtxt{v}: \tview
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Tag] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_1: [\tattr]
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_2: [\tview]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v \vtag{v_1}{v_2}: \tview
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Attr] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_1: \tstr
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_2: \tstr
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v \vattr{v_1}{v_2}: \tattr
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Click] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v: \tone \rightarrow \tone
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v \vclick{v}: \tattr
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=T-Ret] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v: A
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_e \eret{v}: A
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Let] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_e e_1: A_1
  \and
  \Gamma, x: A_1 \semi \Sigma \vdash_e e_2: A_2
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_e \elet{x}{e_1}{e_2}: A_2
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-App] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_1: A_1 \rightarrow A_2
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_2: A_1
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_e v_1\ v_2: A_2
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-New] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v: A
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_e \eref{v}: \tref{A}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Get] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v: \tref{A}
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_e \eget{v}: A
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Set] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_1: \tref{A}
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v_2: A
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_e \eset{v_1}{v_2}: \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=T-Case] {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_v v: [A]
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_e e_1: A'
  \and
  \Gamma, x: A, xs: [A] \semi \Sigma \vdash_e e_2: A'
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Sigma \vdash_e \ecase{v}{[]}{e_1}{x :: xs}{e_2}: A'
}
\end{mathpar}

\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=E-Let] {
  \sigma \semi e_1 \mapsto e_1' \semi \sigma'
} {
  \sigma \semi \elet{x}{e_1}{e_2} 
  \mapsto 
  \elet{x}{e_1'}{e_2} \semi \sigma'
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{align*}
\sigma \semi \elet{x}{\eret{v}}{e}
&\mapsto
e[v/x] \semi \sigma
&&\textsc{E-Ret}\\
\sigma \semi (\lambda x.e)\ v
&\mapsto
e[v/x] \semi \sigma
&&\textsc{E-App}\\
\sigma \semi \eref{v}
&\mapsto
r \semi \sigma[r \mapsto v]
\quad (r\ \mf{fresh})
&&\textsc{E-App}\\
\sigma \semi \eget{r}
&\mapsto
\sigma(r) \semi \sigma
&&\textsc{E-Get}\\
\sigma \semi \eset{r}{v}
&\mapsto
\eret{()} \semi \sigma[r \mapsto v]
&&\textsc{E-Set}\\
\sigma \semi \ecase{[]}{[]}{e_1}{x :: xs}{e_2}
&\mapsto
e_1 \semi \sigma
&&\textsc{E-CaseN}\\
\sigma \semi \ecase{v :: vs}{[]}{e_1}{x :: xs}{e_2}
&\mapsto
e_2[v/x, vs/xs] \semi \sigma
&&\textsc{E-CaseC}\\
\end{align*}

\subsection{Context-Free Translation}

We define a ``context-free'' translation on user-level types, values,
expressions, bodies, and programs.
(This translation is ``context-free'' in that it does not account for
how the system evolves during runtime.)

Interesting cases are \hl{highlighted}.

Types:
\begin{align*}
\llrr{\tone} &= \tone\\
\llrr{\tstr} &= \tstr\\
\llrr{A \xrightarrow{E} A} &= \llrr{A} \rightarrow \llrr{A}\\
\llrr{[A]} &= [\llrr{A}]\\
\llrr{\tview} &= \tview\\
\llrr{\tattr} &= \tattr\\
\mhl{\llrr{\tcomp{A}}} &= A \rightarrow \tref{\tbool} 
\times (\tone \rightarrow \tview)\\
\mhl{\llrr{\tset{A}}} &= A \rightarrow \tone
\end{align*}
Values:
\begin{align*}
\llrr{x} &= x\\
\llrr{()} &= ()\\
\llrr{s} &= s\\
\llrr{\lambda x.e} &= \lambda x.\llrr{e}\\
\llrr{[]} &= []\\
\llrr{v_1 :: v_2} &= \llrr{v_1} :: \llrr{v_2}\\
\mhl{\llrr{\C}} &= x_\C\\
\llrr{\vtxt{v}} &= \vtxt{\llrr{v}}\\
\llrr{\vtag{v_1}{v_2}} &= \vtag{\llrr{v_1}}{\llrr{v_2}}\\
\llrr{\vattr{v_1}{v_2}} &= \vattr{\llrr{v_1}}{\llrr{v_2}}\\
\llrr{\vclick{v}} &= \vclick{\llrr{v}}
\end{align*}
Expressions:
\begin{align*}
\llrr{\eret{v}} &= \eret{\llrr{v}}\\
\llrr{\elet{x}{e_1}{e_2}} &= \elet{x}{\llrr{e_1}}{\llrr{e_2}}\\
\llrr{v\ v} &= \llrr{v}\llrr{v}\\
\llrr{\ecase{v}{[]}{e_1}{x_\mf{hd} :: x_\mf{tl}}{e_2}}
&= \ecase{\llrr{v}}{[]}{\llrr{e_1}}{x_\mf{hd} :: x_\mf{tl}}{\llrr{e_2}}
\end{align*}
Bodies:

We have two different translations on bodies,
corresponding to their interpretations in different phases.
In $\phi=\mf{Init}$, $\mf{useState}$ \emph{creates} a reference cell,
while in $\phi=\mf{Succ}$, $\mf{useState}$ \emph{reads}
from a (previously created) reference cell.

In our translation, the $\mf{Init}$ phase initalizes all references
and returns a \emph{thunk} which closes over those references.
This thunk is then invoked by the runtime to render or re-render
the component in question.

We also track a boolean $\mf{flag}$, which corresponds to the
$\mf{rerender}$ memory field in the source, and is closed
over by any setters.
\begin{align*}
\mhl{\llrr{\hat{e}}} &= \elet{\mf{flag}}{\eref{\mf{false}}}
{\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}}}\\
\llrr{\elet{x}{e}{\hat{e}}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}
&= \elet{x}{\llrr{e}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}}\\
\mhl{\llrr{\estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}}
&= \elet{x_\ell}{\eref{\llrr{v}}}{\\
  &\quad\ \elet{x}{\eget{x_\ell}}{\\
    &\quad\ \elet{x_\mf{set}}{\lambda y.(\eset{x_\ell}{y} 
    \semi \eset{\mf{flag}}{\mf{true}})}{\\
      &\quad\ \llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}}
  }
}\\
\mhl{\llrr{e}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}}
&= (\mf{flag}, \lambda ().\llrr{\hat{e}_0}^\mf{Succ})\\
\llrr{\elet{x}{e}{\hat{e}}}^\mf{Succ}
&= \elet{x}{\llrr{e}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Succ}}\\
\mhl{\llrr{\estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}}}^\mf{Succ}}
&= \elet{x}{\eget{x_\ell}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Succ}}\\
\llrr{e}^\mf{Succ} &= \llrr{e}
\end{align*}

Example:
\begin{align*}
\llrr{
  &\elet{x}{w * w}{\\
    &\estate{y}{\ell_0}{x}{\\
      &\elet{z}{y * y}{\\
          &\eret{\vtag{[]}{\vtxt{z}}}
      }
    }
  }
}\\
&\qquad=\\
&\elet{\mf{flag}}{\eref{false}}{\\
  &\elet{x}{w * w}{\\
    &\elet{y_\ell}{\eref{x}}{\\
      &\elet{y}{\eget{y_\ell}}{\\
        &\elet{y_\mf{set}}{\lambda y.(\eset{x_\ell}{y} 
    \semi \eset{\mf{flag}}{\mf{true}})}{\\
          &\elet{z}{y * y}{\\
            &(\mf{flag}, \lambda ().(\\
              &\quad\elet{x}{w * w}{\\
                &\quad\elet{y}{\eget{y_\ell}}{\\
                  &\quad\elet{z}{y * y}{\\
                    &\quad\eret{\vtag{[]}{\vtxt{z}}}\\
                  }
                }
              }
            &))
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
\end{align*}

Programs:

This approach does \emph{not} allow for 
mutual recursion between components;
forbidding this capability from the source seems much easier
than adding it to the translation/target.
(e.g. \emph{morally} we want to move all the components 
to a let-rec-and block,
but this breaks FGCBV and messes with simulation correspondence.)
\begin{align*}
\llrr{\elet{x}{e}{P}}^P &= \elet{x}{e}{\llrr{P}^P}\\
\mhl{\llrr{\elet{\C(x)}{\hat{e}}{P}}^P} &=
\elet{x_\C}{\lambda x.\llrr{\hat{e}}}{\llrr{P}^P}\\
\llrr{e}^P &= \llrr{e}
\end{align*}

\subsection{Runtime and Context}

The above translation does not let us reason about the
\emph{intermediate stages} of a program's evaluation,
which is necessary to prove a theorem of the form:
$$\mf{if}\ m \semi e \mapsto e' \semi m'
\mf{, then}\ \llrr{m} \semi \llrr{e} \mapsto^* \llrr{e'} \semi \llrr{m'}.$$

For instance, how do we translate the (intermediate-only)
value $\vset{\ell}{p}$?

Consider rule \ts{EB-StI}, which says:
\begin{align*}
\bstep{m}{\estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}}&}{\mf{Init}}{p}
{\hat{e}[v/x, \vset{\ell}{p}/x_\mf{set}]}{m'}
&&\ts{EB-StI}\\
(m' &= m \mid m[p].\mf{st}[\ell] = v)
\end{align*}
Putting aside the issue of translating $m$,
and ignoring the rerender flag (which causes more problems!)
for brevity, we translate
the left-hand expression as follows (for some $\hat{e}_0$):
\begin{align*}
&\elet{x_\ell}{\eref{\llrr{v}}}{\\
  &\elet{x}{\eget{x_\ell}}{\\
    &\elet{x_\mf{set}}{\lambda y.\eset{x_\ell}{y}}{\\
      &\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}
    }
  }
}
\end{align*}
To step this expression, by the semantics of the target language,
$\eref{v}$ generates a \emph{fresh} name
(call it $r$ to distinguish it from source-language labels $\ell$),
and puts $v$ on the heap at $r$.
We get the following:
$$\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}
[r/x_\ell, v/x, \lambda y.\eset{r}{y}/x_\mf{set}]$$
which we want to say is equivalent to 
$\llrr{\hat{e}[v/x,\vset{\ell}{p}/x_\mf{set}]}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}$.

Now clearly, we need to draw \emph{some} kind of correspondence between
$\llrr{\vset{\ell}{p}}$ and $\lambda y.\eset{r}{y}$.
\emph{Morally}, the source location $(p, \ell)$
agrees with the target location $r$,
\emph{but since $r$ is freshly generated,
we have no information linking the two.}
There is no way of translating 
$\llrr{\vset{\ell}{p}}=\lambda y.\eset{r}{y}$
without some additional \emph{context}.

We have a similar problem in translating the following rule:
\begin{align*}
\bstep{m}{\estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}}&}{\mf{Succ}}{p}
{\hat{e}[v'/x, \vset{\ell}{p}/x_\mf{set}]}{m}
&&\ts{EB-StS}\\
(v' &= m[p].\mf{st}[\ell])
\end{align*}
In this case, we don't even know how to translate the left-hand body:
\begin{itemize}
\item Our ``context-free'' translation 
$\llrr{\estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}}}^\mf{Succ}
= \elet{x}{\eget{x_\ell}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Succ}}$
doesn't work, because it leaves $x_\ell$ free,
but in order for this term to step, $x_\ell$ must be bound to
a concrete reference $r$.
\item Furthermore, we don't know \emph{which} such $r$ to use!
Even if we had a map from $(p, \ell) \mapsto r$,
we would \emph{also} need to parameterize our translation
by $p$, since this is given by the operational context
and not the term syntax.
\end{itemize}

\section{RTC-Deltaless}

Removing $\Delta$ (and thus mutual component recursion)
from the source language.

Primary goal: bring source and target languages closer together
by allowing components to be interpreted as let-bound functions,
and avoiding mutual recursion. Gets rid of ``program'' top-level,
whose encoding is not straightforward
and which distracts from the point of useState/components.

Secondary benefits:
\begin{itemize}
\item ``anonymous components'' allow for new expressive patterns
\item should be strongly normalizing
(although this might be hard to prove...)
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Syntax}

Here, $\C$ is a \emph{syntactic token} (like $\lambda$)
instead of a name like before.
\begin{align*}
\text{Value } v &::= x \mid () \mid s 
\mid \lambda x.e \mid \mhl{\C(x).\hat{e}}
\mid [] \mid v :: v\\
&\quad\mid \vtxt{v} \mid \vtag{v}{v}
\mid \vattr{v}{v} \mid \vclick{v}\\
&\quad\mid \mgrey{\pair{v}{v}} \mid \mgrey{\vset{\ell}{p}}\\
\text{Expression } e &::= \eret{v} \mid \elet{x}{e}{e} \mid v\ v \\
&\quad\mid \ecase{v}{[]}{e_1}{x_\mf{hd} :: x_\mf{tl}}{e_2}\\
\text{Body }\hat{e} &::= e \mid \estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}} \mid \elet{x}{e}{\hat{e}}\\
\text{Type } \ptp &::= \tone \mid \tstr \mid \ptp \arrow{E} \ptp
\mid [\ptp] \\
&\quad\mid \tview \mid \tattr
\mid \mhl{\tcomp{\ptp\ \Ell}} \mid \tset{\ptp}\\
\text{Effect } E &::= \epsilon \mid \emut\\
\text{Label-context }\Ell &::= \map{\ell}{\ptp}
\end{align*}

\subsection{Typing}

\subsubsection{Value Typing}

Judgment: $\Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: \ptp$
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TV-Var] {
  \Gamma[x] = \ptp
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash x: \ptp
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TV-Unit] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash (): \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TV-Str] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash s: \tstr
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TV-Lam] {
  \Gamma, x: \ptp_1 \semi \Pi \vdash e: \ptp_2 \mid E
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \lambda x.e: \ptp_1 \xrightarrow{E} \ptp_2
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TV-Comp] {
  \Gamma, x: \ptp \semi \Pi \vdash \hat{e}: \Ell
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \C(x).\hat{e} : \tcomp{\ptp\ \Ell}
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TV-Nil] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash []: [\ptp]
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TV-Cons] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_1: \ptp
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_2: [\ptp]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_1 :: v_2: [\ptp]
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TV-Text] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: \tstr
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \vtxt{v}: \tview
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TV-Tag] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_1: [\tattr]
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_2: [\tview]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \vtag{v_1}{v_2}: \tview
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TV-Attr] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_1: \tstr
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_2: \tstr
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \vattr{v_1}{v_2}: \tattr
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TV-Click] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: \tone \arrow{E} \tone
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \vclick{v}: \tattr
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=\grey{TV-Spec}] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_1: \tcomp{\ptp\ \Ell}
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_2: \ptp
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \pair{v_1}{v_2}: \tview
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=\grey{TV-Set}] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \Pi[p]: \tcomp{\ptp\ \Ell}
  \and
  \ell \in \Pi[p].\mf{labs}
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \vset{\ell}{p}: \Ell[\ell]
}
\end{mathpar}

\subsubsection{Expression Typing}

Judgment: $\Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash e: \ptp \mid E$
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TE-Ret] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: \ptp
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \eret{v}: \ptp \mid \epsilon
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TE-Let] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash e_1: \ptp_1 \mid E
  \and
  \Gamma, x: \ptp_1 \semi \Pi \vdash e_2: \ptp_2 \mid E
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \elet{x}{e_1}{e_2}: \ptp_2 \mid E
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TE-AppL] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_1: \ptp_1 \arrow{E} \ptp_2
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_2: \ptp_1
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_1\ v_2: \ptp_2 \mid E
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TE-AppC] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_1: \tcomp{\ptp\ \Ell}
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_2: \ptp
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_1\ v_2: \tview \mid \epsilon
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TE-AppS] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_1: \tset{\ptp}
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_2: \ptp
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v_1\ v_2: \tone \mid \emut
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TE-Case] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: [\ptp_1]
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash e_1: \ptp_2 \mid E
  \\
  \Gamma, x_\mf{hd}: \ptp_1, x_\mf{tl}: [\ptp_1] \semi \Pi
  \vdash e_2: \ptp_2 \mid E
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash
  \ecase{v}{[]}{e_1}{x_\mf{hd} :: x_\mf{tl}}{e_2}: \ptp_2 \mid E
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TE-Sub] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash e: \ptp \mid \epsilon
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash e: \ptp \mid \emut
}
\end{mathpar}

\subsubsection{Body Typing}

Judgment: $\Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \hat{e}: \Ell$
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TB-Lift] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash e: \tview \mid \epsilon
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash e: \emptyset
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TB-Let] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash e: \ptp \mid \epsilon
  \and
  \Gamma, x: \ptp \semi \Pi \vdash \hat{e}: \Ell
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \elet{x}{e}{\hat{e}}: \emptyset
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TB-State] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: \ptp
  \and
  \Gamma, x: \ptp, x_\mf{set}: \tset{\ptp} \semi \Pi
  \vdash \hat{e}: \Ell
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}}: \Ell, \ell: \ptp
}
\end{mathpar}

\subsection{Runtime}

\subsubsection{Syntax}

\begin{align*}
\text{Runtime values } u
&::= x \mid () \mid [] \mid u :: u
\mid \elit{v} \mid \enode{id}{v}{u}
\mid p\\
\text{Runtime expressions } \rho
&::= \eret{u} \mid \elet{x}{\rho}{\rho}\\
&\quad\mid \einit{v} \mid \einits{v} \mid \escan{v} \mid \escans{v}\\
&\quad\mid \echk{u} \mid \echks{u}
\mid \esearch{id}{u} \mid \esearches{id}{u}\\
&\quad\mid \eeval{\phi}{p}{\hat{e}} \mid \enew{\phi}{p}{u}
\mid \ehdl{e} \mid \eprog{e}\\
\text{Runtime type } \rtp &::= \tone \mid \ttree
\mid [\ttree]
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{Typing}

Values:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Var] {
  \Gamma[x] = \rtp
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash x: \rtp
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Unit] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash (): \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Nil] { } {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash []: [\ttree]
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Lit] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: \tstr
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash \elit{v}: \ttree
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Cons] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash u_1: \ttree
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash u_2: [\ttree]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash u_1 :: u_2: [\ttree]
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Node] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: [\tattr]
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash u: [\ttree]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash \enode{id}{v}{u}: \ttree
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Path] {
  \Pi[p].\mf{tree} = \tt
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash p: \ttree
}
\end{mathpar}

Expressions:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=TR-Ret] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash u: \rtp
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \eret{u}: \rtp
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Let] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \rho_1: \rtp_1
  \and
  \Gamma, x: \rtp_1 \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \rho_2: \rtp_2
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \elet{x}{\rho_1}{\rho_2}: \rtp_2
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Init] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: \tview
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \einit{v}: \ttree
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Inits] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: [\tview]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \einits{v}: [\ttree]
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Scan] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: \tview
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \escan{v}: \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Scans] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: [\tview]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \escans{v}: \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Chk] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash u: \ttree
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \echk{v}: \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Chks] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash u: [\ttree]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \echks{v}: \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Sr] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash u: \ttree
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \esearch{id}{v}: \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Srs] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash u: [\ttree]
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \esearches{id}{v}: \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-EvalI] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \Pi[p]: \tcomp{\ptp\ \Ell_0}
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \hat{e}: \Ell
  \and
  \Ell_0 = \Ell \cup \Pi[p].\mf{labs}
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \eeval{\mf{Init}}{p}{\hat{e}}: \tview
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-EvalS] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \Pi[p]: \tcomp{\ptp\ \Ell_0}
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \hat{e}: \Ell
  \and
  \Ell \subseteq \Pi[p].\mf{labs} = \Ell_0
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \eeval{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\hat{e}}: \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-NewI] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \Pi[p]: \tcomp{\ptp\ \Ell_0}
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash u: \ttree
  \and
  \Ell_0 = \Pi[p].\mf{labs}
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{u}: \tview
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-NewS] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash \Pi[p]: \tcomp{\ptp\ \Ell_0}
  \and
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash u: \ttree
  \and
  \Ell_0 = \Pi[p].\mf{labs}
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{u}: \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Hdl] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash e: \tone \mid E
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \ehdl{e}: \tone
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=TR-Prog] {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash e: \tview \mid \epsilon
} {
  \Gamma \semi \Pi \Vdash_\rho \eprog{e}: \tone
}
\end{mathpar}

\subsubsection{FGCBV Semantics}

Admin:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-Let] {
  m \semi \rho_1 \hookrightarrow \rho_1' \semi m'
} {
  m \semi \elet{x}{\rho_1}{\rho_2}
  \hookrightarrow
  \elet{x}{\rho_1'}{\rho_2} \semi m'
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{align*}
m \semi \elet{x}{\eret{u}}{\rho}
&\hookrightarrow
\rho[u/x] \semi m
&&\ts{ER-Ret}\\
\end{align*}

Initialization:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-ITag] {
  x\ \mf{fresh}
  \and
  \mf{id}\ \mf{fresh}
} {
  m \semi \einit{\vtag{v_1}{v_2}}
  \hookrightarrow
  \elet{x}{\einits{v_2}}{\eret{\enode{id}{v_1}{x}}} \semi m
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=ER-ISpec] {
  p\ \mf{fresh}
  \\
  m'=m[p \mapsto \{
    \mf{spec}=\pair{\C(x).\hat{e}}{v}, 
    \mf{st}: \cdot, 
    \mf{tree}: \cdot, 
    \mf{rerender}: \mf{ff}
  \}]
} {
  m \semi \einit{\pair{\C(x).\hat{e}}{v}}
  \hookrightarrow
  \eeval{\mf{Init}}{p}{\hat{e}[v/x]} \semi m'
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{align*}
m \semi \einit{\vtxt{v}}
&\hookrightarrow
\eret{\elit{v}} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-ILit}\\
m \semi \einits{[]}
&\hookrightarrow
\eret{[]} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-INil}\\
m \semi \einits{v :: vs}
&\hookrightarrow
(\elet{x}{\einit{v}}{\\&\quad
  \elet{xs}{\einits{vs}}{&&\ts{ER-ICons}\\&\quad
    \eret{x :: xs}
  }
}) \semi m
\end{align*}

Component Evaluation:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-Eval] {
  \bstep{m}{\hat{e}}{\phi}{p}{\hat{e}'}{m'}
} {
  m \semi \eeval{\phi}{p}{\hat{e}}
  \hookrightarrow
  \eeval{\phi}{p}{\hat{e}'} \semi m'
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{align*}
m \semi \eeval{\phi}{p}{\eret{v}}
&\hookrightarrow
\elet{x}{\einit{v}}{\enew{\phi}{p}{x}} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-New}\\
m \semi \enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{u}
&\hookrightarrow
p \semi m \mid m[p].\mf{tree} = u
&&\ts{ER-NewI}\\
m \semi \enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{u}
&\hookrightarrow
() \semi m \mid m[p].\mf{tree} = u
&&\ts{ER-NewS}\\
\end{align*}

Event Handling:
\begin{align*}
m \semi \esearch{id}{\elit{v}}
&\hookrightarrow
\eret{()} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-SLit}\\
m \semi \esearch{id}{p}
&\hookrightarrow
\esearch{id}{m[p].\mf{tree}} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-SPath}\\
m \semi \esearch{id}{\enode{id'}{v}{u}}
&\hookrightarrow
\esearches{id}{u} \semi m\quad (\mf{id} \neq \mf{id}')
&&\ts{ER-SNodeF}\\
m \semi \esearch{id}{\enode{id}{v}{u}}
&\hookrightarrow
\elet{\_}{\escans{v}}{\esearches{id}{u}} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-SNodeT}\\
m \semi \esearches{id}{[]}
&\hookrightarrow
\eret{()} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-SNil}\\
m \semi \esearches{id}{u :: us}
&\hookrightarrow
\elet{\_}{\esearch{id}{u}}{\esearch{id}{us}} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-SCons}\\
\end{align*}
\begin{align*}
m \semi \escan{\vattr{v_1}{v_2}}
&\hookrightarrow
\eret{()} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-ScAttr}\\
m \semi \escan{\vclick{v}}
&\hookrightarrow
\ehdl{v\ ()} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-ScClick}\\
m \semi \escans{[]}
&\hookrightarrow
\eret{()} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-ScNil}\\
m \semi \escans{u :: us}
&\hookrightarrow
\elet{\_}{\escan{u}}{\escans{us}} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-ScCons}\\
\end{align*}
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-Hdl] {
  \estep{m}{e}{e'}{m'}
} {
  m \semi \ehdl{e} \hookrightarrow \ehdl{e'} \semi m'
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{align*}
m \semi \ehdl{\eret{()}} 
&\hookrightarrow
\eret{()} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-HDone}\\
\end{align*}

Checking:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-ChkPF] {
  m[p].\mf{rerender} = \ff
} {
  m \semi \echk{p}
  \hookrightarrow
  \echk{m[p].\mf{tree}} \semi m
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=ER-ChkPT] {
  m[p].\mf{rerender} = \tt
  \and
  m[p].\mf{spec} = \pair{\C(x).\hat{e}}{v}
} {
  m \semi \echk{p}
  \hookrightarrow
  \eeval{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\hat{e}[v/x]}
  \semi m \mid m[p].\mf{rerender} = \ff
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{align*}
m \semi \echk{\elit{v}}
&\hookrightarrow
\eret{()} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-ChkLit}\\
m \semi \echk{\enode{id}{v}{u}}
&\hookrightarrow
\echks{u} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-ChkNode}\\
m \semi \echks{[]} &\hookrightarrow \eret{()} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-ChkNil}\\
m \semi \echks{u :: us}
&\hookrightarrow
\elet{\_}{\echk{u}}{\echks{us}}
&&\ts{ER-ChkCons}
\end{align*}

Programs:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-Prog] {
  \cdot \semi e \mapsto e' \semi \cdot
} {
  \cdot \semi \eprog{e} \mapsto \eprog{e'} \semi \cdot
}
\and
\inferrule*[right=ER-PDone] {
  m' = m[p_0 \mapsto \{\mf{spec}: \pair{\C(\_).\eret{v}}{()},
  \mf{st}: \cdot,
  \mf{tree}: \cdot, \mf{rerender}: \mf{ff}\}]
} {
  m \semi \eprog{\eret{v}}
  \hookrightarrow
  \elet{x}{\einit{v}}{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p_0}{x}}
  \semi m'
}
\end{mathpar}

Top-Level:
\begin{mathpar}
\inferrule*[right=ER-Top] {
  m \semi \rho \hookrightarrow \rho' \semi m'
} {
  m \semi \rho \hookrightarrow_0 \rho' \semi m'
}
\end{mathpar}
\begin{align*}
m \semi \eret{()} 
&\hookrightarrow
\elet{\_}{\esearch{id}{p_0}}{\echk{p_0}} \semi m
&&\ts{ER-Event}
\end{align*}

\section{Reference-Abstract Translation}

\subsection{Interpretations}

We define our translation with respect to a series of
\emph{reference interpretation functions},
which let us map between memory locations
in the source and target languages.

Each such function is a \emph{partial} map
from some domain in the source language
to the set of locations in the target language.

In particular, we have four such functions
(where $P$, $L$, and $R$ are the domains
of paths, labels, and target-langauge references,
respectively):
\begin{itemize}
\item $\mathbb{S} : P \rightharpoonup R$ maps paths to ``specs''
(of type $\tref{\tone \rightarrow \tview}$),
\item $\mathbb{T}  : P \rightharpoonup R$ maps paths to trees
(of type $\tref{\ttree}$),
\item $\mathbb{B}  : P \rightharpoonup R$ maps paths to rerender flags
(of type $\tref{\tbool}$), and
\item $\mathbb{L} : P \times L \rightharpoonup R$ maps path/label pairs
to the corresponding state cell (of varying type).
\end{itemize}

Call the four-tuple
$\mathbb{I}=\four{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{T}}{\mathbb{L}}{\mathbb{B}}$
an \emph{interpretation}. We implicitly parametrize all of our
translations by an interpretation.

Furthermore, call $\mathbb{I}$ a \emph{valid} interpretation
(of a particular translation- e.g. $\llrr{v}$, $\llrr{\Pi}$, etc.)
if all instances of interpretation functions are well-defined.
For instance, if $\mathbb{L}(p, \ell)$ appears in the translation,
then $\mathbb{L}(p, \ell) \neq \bot$.

We can now state theorems such as the following:

\begin{theorem}[Type preservation, values.]
If $\Gamma \semi \Pi \vdash v: A$,
then, for any valid interpretation $\mathbb{I}$,
we get $\llrr{\Gamma} \semi \llrr{\Pi} \vdash \llrr{v}: \llrr{A}$.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Translation}

Types:
\begin{align*}
\llrr{\tone} &= \tone\\
\llrr{\tstr} &= \tstr\\
\llrr{A \xrightarrow{E} A} &= \llrr{A} \rightarrow \llrr{A}\\
\llrr{[A]} &= [\llrr{A}]\\
\llrr{\tview} &= \tview\\
\llrr{\tattr} &= \tattr\\
\mhl{\llrr{\tcomp{A\ \Ell}}} &= A \rightarrow \tref{\tbool} 
\times (\tone \rightarrow \tview)\\
\mhl{\llrr{\tset{A}}} &= A \rightarrow \tone
\end{align*}
Values:
\begin{align*}
\llrr{x} &= x\\
\llrr{()} &= ()\\
\llrr{s} &= s\\
\llrr{\lambda x.e} &= \lambda x.\llrr{e}\\
\mhl{\llrr{\C(x).\hat{e}}} &= 
\lambda x.\llrr{\hat{e}}^\top\\
\llrr{[]} &= []\\
\llrr{v_1 :: v_2} &= \llrr{v_1} :: \llrr{v_2}\\
\llrr{\vtxt{v}} &= \vtxt{\llrr{v}}\\
\llrr{\vtag{v_1}{v_2}} &= \vtag{\llrr{v_1}}{\llrr{v_2}}\\
\llrr{\vattr{v_1}{v_2}} &= \vattr{\llrr{v_1}}{\llrr{v_2}}\\
\llrr{\vclick{v}} &= \vclick{\llrr{v}}\\
\mhl{\llrr{\pair{v_1}{v_2}}} 
&= \mf{spec}\ \lambda().\llrr{v_1} \llrr{v_2}\\
\mhl{\llrr{\vset{\ell}{p}}}
&= \lambda x. \eset{\mathbb{L}(p, \ell)}{x}
\semi \eset{\mathbb{B}(p)}{\mf{true}}
\end{align*}
Expressions:
\begin{align*}
\llrr{\eret{v}} &= \eret{\llrr{v}}\\
\llrr{\elet{x}{e_1}{e_2}} &= \elet{x}{\llrr{e_1}}{\llrr{e_2}}\\
\llrr{v\ v} &= \llrr{v}\llrr{v}\\
\llrr{\ecase{v}{[]}{e_1}{x_\mf{hd} :: x_\mf{tl}}{e_2}}
&= \ecase{\llrr{v}}{[]}{\llrr{e_1}}{x_\mf{hd} :: x_\mf{tl}}{\llrr{e_2}}
\end{align*}
Bodies (contextless):
\begin{align*}
\mhl{\llrr{\hat{e}}}^\top &= \elet{\mf{flag}}{\eref{\mf{false}}}
{\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}}}\\
\llrr{\elet{x}{e}{\hat{e}}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}
&= \elet{x}{\llrr{e}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}}\\
\mhl{\llrr{\estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}}
&= \elet{x_\ell}{\eref{\llrr{v}}}{\\
  &\quad\ \elet{x}{\eget{x_\ell}}{\\
    &\quad\ \elet{x_\mf{set}}{\lambda y.(\eset{x_\ell}{y} 
    \semi \eset{\mf{flag}}{\mf{true}})}{\\
      &\quad\ \llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}}
  }
}\\
\mhl{\llrr{e}^\mf{Init}_{\hat{e}_0}}
&= (\mf{flag}, \lambda ().\llrr{\hat{e}_0}^\mf{Succ})\\
\llrr{\elet{x}{e}{\hat{e}}}^\mf{Succ}
&= \elet{x}{\llrr{e}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Succ}}\\
\mhl{\llrr{\estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}}}^\mf{Succ}}
&= \elet{x}{\eget{x_\ell}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Succ}}\\
\llrr{e}^\mf{Succ} &= \llrr{e}
\end{align*}
Bodies (context-sensitive) \hl{IN PROGRESS}:
\begin{align*}
\llrr{\elet{x}{e}{\hat{e}}}^{\mf{Init}(\hat{e}_0)}_p
&= \elet{x}{\llrr{e}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^{\mf{Init}(\hat{e}_0)}_p}\\
\mhl{\llrr{\estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}}}^{\mf{Init}(\hat{e}_0)}_p}
&= \elet{x_\ell}{\eref{\llrr{v}}}{\\
  &\quad\ \elet{x}{\eget{x_\ell}}{\\
    &\quad\ \elet{x_\mf{set}}{\lambda y.\\
      &\qquad(\eset{x_\ell}{y} \semi \eset{\mathbb{B}(p)}{\mf{true}})
      \\&\quad\ 
    }{\llrr{\hat{e}}^{\mf{Init}(\hat{e}_0)}_p}
  }
}\\
\mhl{\llrr{e}^{\mf{Init}(\hat{e}_0)}_p}
&= (\mathbb{B}(p), \lambda ().\llrr{\hat{e}_0}^\mf{Succ}_p)\\
\llrr{\elet{x}{e}{\hat{e}}}^\mf{Succ}_p
&= \elet{x}{\llrr{e}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Succ}_p}\\
\mhl{\llrr{\estate{x}{\ell}{v}{\hat{e}}}^\mf{Succ}_p}
&= \elet{x}{\eget{\mathbb{L}(p, \ell)}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^\mf{Succ}}\\
\llrr{e}^\mf{Succ} &= \llrr{e}
\end{align*}

Runtime values:
\begin{align*}
\llrr{x} &= x\\
\llrr{()} &= ()\\
\llrr{[]} &= []\\
\llrr{u :: u} &= \llrr{u} :: \llrr{u}\\
\llrr{\elit{v}} &= \elit{\llrr{v}}\\
\llrr{\enode{id}{v}{u}} &= \enode{id}{\llrr{v}}{\llrr{u}}\\
\mhl{\llrr{p}} &= \mf{path}(\mathbb{S}(p), \mathbb{T}(p), \mathbb{B}(p))
\end{align*}

Runtime expressions \hl{IN PROGRESS}:
\begin{align*}
\llrr{\eret{u}} &= \eret{\llrr{u}}\\
\llrr{\elet{x}{\rho}{\rho}} &= \elet{x}{\llrr{\rho}}{\llrr{\rho}}\\
\llrr{\einit{v}} &= \mf{init}\ \llrr{v}\\
\llrr{\einits{v}} &= \mf{map}\ \mf{init}\ \llrr{v}\\
\llrr{\escan{v}} &= \mf{scan}\ \llrr{v}\\
\llrr{\escans{v}} &= \mf{iter}\ \mf{scan}\ \llrr{v}\\
\llrr{\echk{u}} &= \mf{check}\ \llrr{u}\\
\llrr{\echks{u}} &= \mf{iter}\ \mf{check}\ \llrr{u}\\
\llrr{\esearch{id}{u}} &= \mf{search}\ \mf{id}\ \llrr{u}\\
\llrr{\esearches{id}{u}} &= \mf{iter}\ (\mf{search}\ \mf{id})\ \llrr{u}\\
\llrr{\eeval{\mf{Init}}{p}{\hat{e}}}
&= \elet{\mf{view}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^{\mf{Init}(\mhl{\hat{e}_0})}_p}{\\
  &\quad\ \elet{\mf{tree}}{\mf{init}\ \mf{view}}{
    \llrr{\enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{\mf{tree}}}
  }
}\\
\llrr{\eeval{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\hat{e}}}
&= \elet{\mf{view}}{\llrr{\hat{e}}^{\mf{Succ}}_p}{\\
  &\quad\ \elet{\mf{tree}}{\mf{init}\ \mf{view}}{
    \llrr{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{\mf{tree}}}
  }
}\\
\llrr{\enew{\mf{Init}}{p}{u}}
&= \eset{\mathbb{T}(p)}{\llrr{u}} \semi \eret{\llrr{p}}\\
\llrr{\enew{\mf{Succ}}{p}{u}}
&= \eset{\mathbb{T}(p)}{\llrr{u}}\\
\llrr{\ehdl{e}} &= \llrr{e}\\
\llrr{\eprog{e}} &= \elet{\mf{view}}{\llrr{e}}{\\
  &\quad\ \elet{b}{\eref{\mf{false}}}{\\
    &\quad\ \elet{s}{\eref{\lambda ().\llrr{e}}}{\\
      &\quad\ \elet{t}{\eref{\mf{view}}}{
        \mhl{()}
      }
    }
  }
}
\end{align*}

Memory:

\begin{align*}\llrr{\pi}^p &= \{\mathbb{B}(p) \mapsto \llrr{\pi.\mf{rerender}}\}
\cup \bigcup_{\ell} \{\mathbb{L}(p, \ell) \mapsto \llrr{\pi.\mf{st}[\ell]}\}\\
&\quad\cup \begin{cases}
\{\mathbb{S}(p) \mapsto \llrr{\pi.\mf{spec}},
  \mathbb{T}(p) \mapsto \llrr{\pi.\mf{tree}}
\} &\pi.\mf{tree}\ \text{defined}\\
\emptyset &\text{otherwise}
\end{cases}\\
\llrr{m} &= \bigcup_p \llrr{m[p]}^p
\end{align*}

Memory typing:
\begin{align*}
\llrr{\Pi} &= \bigcup_p \{\mathbb{B}(p) \mapsto \tbool\}
\cup \bigcup_\ell \{\mathbb{L}(p, \ell) \mapsto \llrr{\Ell[\ell]}\}\\
&\quad\cup \begin{cases}
\{\mathbb{S}(p) \mapsto \tone \rightarrow \tview,
  \mathbb{T}(p) \mapsto \ttree
\} &\pi.\mf{tree}\ \text{defined}\\
\emptyset &\text{otherwise}
\end{cases}\\
&\qquad(\text{where}\ \Pi[p].\mf{spec} = \pair{A}{\Ell})
\end{align*}

\end{document}
